# Verified products: vascular role (Prelox replacements)

**Scope:** Products that could take over Life Extension Prelox's blood-flow role: certified arginine and citrulline singles, licensed Prelox, and Pycnogenol add-backs.

**Method:** Each product was checked by a verification agent (label and directions, certification in the certifier's own database, regulatory record, dose against human trials, safety, interactions, who should avoid it, stop signals) and then audited by two challenge agents, one on certification and label claims and one on safety and evidence. Captures date from 7–8 November 2026. Records are listed in the order of `reports/Prelox and Performer 8 replacements.md`.

## Takeaway

- No certified Prelox-type combination and no certified Pycnogenol single exist; the rebuild is a certified amino acid plus 80–100 mg of trademarked Pycnogenol.
- NOW Sports L-Arginine 1,000 mg tablets and Kaged Citrulline were confirmed on Informed Sport's live pages (8 Nov 2026); NOW Arginine & Citrulline powder only through a search-engine copy and the certifier's November 2026 list.
- Pharma Nord Prelox at four tablets a day equals the trial regimen but is uncertified, UK/EU only, and four tablets runs past its maintenance label.
- PODIUM L-Citrulline lost its Informed Sport certification on 29 Sep 2026.

## Gaps

- FDA's warning-letter, recall and tainted-product pages and the Health Canada, TGA and MHRA databases were blocked or unreachable for most products, so a missing regulatory action means "not checked", not "clean".
- Several certifier pages were seen only through search-engine copies; the report marks these as indirect, and the live page must be checked against the bottle's lot number.
- Informed Sport and NSF Certified for Sport do not publish whether their panels cover PDE5 drugs (sildenafil, tadalafil and analogues); BSCG's published menu does.
- No prices or costs were collected, by design.

## Records

### Report rank 1: NOW Foods (NOW Sports) — NOW Sports L-Arginine 1,000 mg Double Strength tablets (60, 120 or 180)

- **Verifier verdict:** recommend — A single fully disclosed ingredient with a directly confirmed Informed Sport listing and batches tested as recently as 9 October 2026, in a tablet format like Prelox, where 3 tablets a day reaches the 2.5-3 g dose of positive ED trials (one tablet above the label, so clear it with a clinician).
- **Product as verified:** NOW Foods (NOW Sports) — NOW Sports L-Arginine 1,000 mg Double Strength tablets (60, 120 or 180)
- **Product page:** https://www.nowfoods.com/products/sports-nutrition/l-arginine-1000-mg-double-strength-tablets
- **Where sold:** US/Canada (Vitacost, Sam's Club, Well.ca, Vitasave). Informed Sport lists the product for Europe and North America. Saudi Arabia: Nahdi (180 tablets) and Soukare (120 tablets). A Nahdi UAE 120-tablet listing showed an expiry date already past (May 2025) and may be stale.
- **Label verified:** yes. Supplement Facts and suggested use as reproduced by Professional Supplement Center and Vitacost (US), Vitasave (Canada) and Nahdi (Saudi, Arabic page), all consistent; NOW's own page and NIH DSLD could not be opened this session. A Canadian listing calls the 120-count a capsule; US listings say tablet.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving size 1 tablet: L-arginine 1 g (1,000 mg) from 1,250 mg L-arginine HCl. Suggested use: 1 tablet twice daily as needed, between meals or at bedtime with at least 8 oz of water or juice (= 2 g/day). Label cautions: adults only, not for pregnant or nursing women; the Canadian label says to avoid with glaucoma or herpes simplex unless prescribed; Nahdi's Arabic page says not to use after a heart attack or with coronary artery disease. Other ingredients were not captured. For a Prelox replacement: 2 tablets match Prelox's arginine content; 3 tablets (3 g/day) reach the positive-trial range, one tablet above the label, so get a clinician's agreement.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** Label dose 2 g/day sits between the ineffective 1.5 g/day (Klotz 1999) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10743698/] and the lowest positive 2.5 g/day (Gallo 2020: IIEF-EF +3.1 overall, +5.4 in mild ED, 12 weeks) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/]. 3 tablets = 3 g/day (El-Wakeel 2020, with sildenafil, 8 weeks) [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31267684]; 5 tablets = 5 g/day (Chen 1999: double-blind, 50 men with organic ED, 6 weeks, 31% vs 12% reported significant improvement) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10233492/]; 6 tablets = 6 g/day (Menafra 2022: 2 g three times daily after meals for 3 months, 98 men with vasculogenic ED, IIEF-6 median 20 to 24 vs unchanged on placebo) [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3]. Meta-analysis range 1.5-5 g/day, OR 3.37 [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/30770070]. Against Prelox: Prelox's trial dose of 2.8-3 g L-arginine aspartate [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/] contains about 1.6-1.7 g arginine (my molecular-weight calculation), so 2 tablets already exceed it, but there is no Pycnogenol.
- **Certification:** Informed Sport. Listing 'L-ARGININE 1000mg tabs' (Now Foods), read directly from the certifier's database on 8 Nov 2026: 'Certified Product'; date certified 26-Jan-2023; regions Europe and North America; 15 batches listed, latest 3480646 tested 09-Oct-2026 (expires 30-Sep-2030) and 3480647 tested 02-Oct-2026, then 25-Jun-2026, 12-Jun-2026, 06-Feb-2026, 17-Oct-2025, 07-Oct-2025, 24-Jul-2025, 02-Jul-2025, 02-Jun-2025, 16-Jan-2025, 20-May-2024 (two), 23-Jan-2023 and 18-Jan-2023. This is the most recent batch testing of any product in this batch. Informed Sport tests every batch before release for more than 285 substances banned in sport using ISO 17025 methods [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice]; it is a drug-screening programme, but whether its panel includes PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil-type drugs) was not confirmed, whereas BSCG's published menu names tadalafil, vardenafil and many sildenafil analogues [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725]. Certification is lot-specific: match the batch number on the container to the database. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/now-foods/l-arginine-1000mg-tabs; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** NOW Foods / NOW Health Group (US manufacturer). Found: a 2005 FDA warning letter over possible quintozene (pesticide) contamination of one lot of American ginseng [https://www.supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/now-responds-to-fda-warning-letter]; a 2016 voluntary recall of six unrelated supplements (about 165 units) after a label-printer error [https://www.Drugs.com/fda/now-health-group-dietary-supplements-recall-mislabeling-13846.html]; an earlier voluntary recall of a phosphatidylserine product that did not meet its label claim [https://wholefoodsmagazine.com/keywords/12602-recall]; and, per a recall aggregator, 2025 food recalls of NOW Nutritional Yeast Powder (Class III, May 2025, terminated) and organic raw cashews (Class II, Sept 2025) [https://recallcheck.net/brand/now-foods; https://recallcheck.net/food-recalls/5197]. None found for any NOW arginine or citrulline product, and the searches surfaced no FDA hidden-drug (tainted product) notice for NOW, although FDA's tainted-products database itself was not queried. Searched: web search 'NOW Foods recall OR warning letter FDA 2024 2025 2026 supplement' (8 Nov 2026). Not searched: FDA's warning-letter and enforcement-report databases directly, Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. Live re-checks were impossible in this pass (WebSearch budget exhausted; fda.gov, sport.wetestyoutrust.com, nowfoods.com, kaged.com, DSLD and Europe PMC blocked; Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC), so these findings rest on the workflow's 8 Nov 2026 discovery reads and the project's earlier notes.
- **Evidence summary:** L-arginine is the supplement with the most ED evidence: 10-RCT meta-analysis (540 men, 1.5-5 g/day) OR 3.37 for improvement, adverse effects 8.3% vs 2.3%, none severe [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/30770070]; Menafra 2022 placebo-controlled RCT at 6 g/day [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3]; Gallo 2020 open-label 2.5 g/day, benefit concentrated in mild ED [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/]; 2024 network meta-analysis found arginine the only single nutraceutical with significant efficacy, ranking below sildenafil and tadalafil [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/39279185]; EAU gives a weak recommendation for mild ED [https://uroweb.org/guidelines/sexual-and-reproductive-health/chapter/management-of-erectile-dysfunction]. Effects are modest (around the 4-point IIEF minimal important difference at best) and no arginine-alone trial ran beyond 3 months.

**Safety risks**

- Post-heart-attack mortality signal: in VINTAGE MI (L-arginine 3 g three times daily = 9 g/day for 6 months after a first STEMI) 6 arginine patients (8.6%) died vs none on placebo and enrolment was closed; authors: 'L-arginine should not be recommended following acute myocardial infarction' [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/]. Mayo and Cleveland Clinic both say not to use after a recent heart attack [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Lowers blood pressure; with nitrates the drop can be dangerous, and it adds to blood-pressure drugs and PDE5 inhibitors [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Gastrointestinal effects (nausea, abdominal pain, diarrhea, bloating) [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; single doses of 3-6 g rarely provoke them, most occur at single doses above 9 g and divided doses help [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17513449/].
- Other reported effects: gout, headache, allergic reactions, airway inflammation or worse asthma [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; insomnia in 5% at 2.5 g/day [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/].
- Long-term safety: NIH ODS says up to 9 g/day is safe and well tolerated for several days or weeks, with GI upset and slightly lower blood pressure at 9-30 g/day [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/]; studies have lasted up to about 3 months [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-Consumer/].
- High potassium if combined with potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Label cautions on glaucoma and herpes simplex (Canada) and on heart attack or coronary artery disease (Nahdi Arabic page) [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets; https://www.nahdionline.com/ar-sa/now-foods-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets/pdp/101506676].
- No stimulants, hormones or botanicals; single amino acid not marketed for sexual enhancement.

**Interactions**

- Nitrates (nitroglycerin, isosorbide mononitrate/dinitrate): do not combine; blood pressure may fall too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine]; Drugs.com: arginine may potentiate nitrates and other NO donors [https://www.drugs.com/npp/l-arginine.html].
- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil): Mayo warns sildenafil plus L-arginine may drop blood pressure too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; Drugs.com rates arginine + sildenafil as a moderate interaction and advises monitoring blood pressure and heart rate [https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine-with-sildenafil-232-0-2061-0.html?professional=1]; deliberate combination trials found more dyspepsia/gastritis but no significant rise in adverse events overall [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31267684; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/33587304].
- Antihypertensives (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, beta-blockers, calcium-channel blockers, alpha-blockers such as doxazosin and prazosin; tamsulosin rated moderate by Drugs.com): additive blood-pressure lowering [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/l-arginine-with-tamsulosin-232-4042-2146-0.html?professional=1].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (e.g., warfarin, aspirin): may increase bleeding risk [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Diabetes drugs: L-arginine may lower blood sugar so doses may need adjusting [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; effect with insulin is unpredictable [https://www.drugs.com/npp/l-arginine.html].
- Potassium-sparing diuretics (amiloride, spironolactone, triamterene): do not combine, risk of hyperkalemia [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Isoproterenol: blood pressure may fall too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction is listed by Mayo Clinic or Cleveland Clinic [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone with a recent heart attack (VINTAGE MI; Mayo; Cleveland Clinic) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with coronary artery disease or heart failure unless a cardiologist approves (NOW's Arabic L-arginine tablet page on Nahdi says not to use with coronary artery disease or after a heart attack; Cleveland Clinic lists heart failure as a serious reaction) [https://www.nahdionline.com/ar-sa/now-foods-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets/pdp/101506676; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Men who take nitrates, or potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with low blood pressure, or on several blood-pressure drugs or a PDE5 inhibitor, unless a clinician supervises [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- People with asthma or significant allergies (may worsen) [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- People with recurrent herpes simplex or glaucoma should ask a physician first (caution printed on NOW's Canadian L-arginine tablet label) [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets].
- Guanidinoacetate methyltransferase deficiency; adults over 65 without provider approval [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Men with new, unexplained ED who have not had a cardiovascular check, because ED predicts cardiovascular events [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16414947/].

**Stop signals**

- Dizziness, light-headedness or fainting (blood pressure too low).
- Chest pain, breathlessness or new palpitations: stop and seek emergency care.
- Hives, facial or throat swelling, or wheezing: stop and seek urgent care [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Persistent diarrhea, nausea, bloating or heartburn; a gout flare; worsening asthma [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- A cold-sore (herpes) outbreak, given the herpes caution on NOW's Canadian arginine label [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets].
- Being prescribed nitrates, a potassium-sparing diuretic or a new blood-pressure drug: stop and review with the prescriber.
- No noticeable benefit after about 3 months (the length of the longest arginine-alone trials) [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3].

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - Directly confirmed from the raw 8 Nov 2026 capture (live fetch, HTTP 200) of https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/now-foods/l-arginine-1000mg-tabs: 'Certified Product', tablet, certified 26-Jan-2023, regions Europe and North America, 15 batches with latest 3480646 tested 09-Oct-2026 (expires 30-Sep-2030). Every batch number and test date in the shortlist matches.
  - The listing pictures only the 60- and 180-tablet bottles. The 120-count sold in Canada and Saudi Arabia is not pictured and one Canadian listing calls it a capsule, so a 120-count bottle counts as certified only if its batch number is on the list.
  - Two listed batches (3275655 and 3275569) expire on 30-Nov-2026.
  - Label (1 g arginine from 1,250 mg arginine HCl per tablet; 1 tablet twice daily) rests on retailer copies; NOW's page and DSLD were not readable. No reformulation evidence.
  - Informed Sport and NSF test for substances banned in sport and do not publish PDE5-inhibitor coverage; only BSCG's menu explicitly lists sildenafil/tadalafil analogues (https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725).

- *safety-evidence lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - The listing matches the raw 8-Nov-2026 capture: certified 26-Jan-2023, 15 batches, latest 3480646 tested 09-Oct-2026. But Informed Sport gives regional availability as Europe and North America only, so a bottle bought in Saudi Arabia or the UAE (Nahdi, Soukare) must be matched to a listed batch ID before relying on the certification.
  - PDE5 caveat: Informed Sport screens for sport-banned substances. Sildenafil-type drugs are not WADA-prohibited and it is unconfirmed whether they are tested; BSCG adds them as non-sport drugs. The practical risk is low for a single amino acid from a major maker.
  - The evidence is slightly overstated. Rhim 2019's OR 3.37 mixes arginine-only and combination trials and carries a published correction. Placebo-controlled support for arginine alone comes mainly from Menafra 2022 (6 g/day; median IIEF-6 gain of 4 points, about the minimal clinically important difference) and Chen 1999 (5 g/day).
  - Missed interaction (inference): ACE inhibitors and ARBs also raise potassium. Extend the potassium-sparing-diuretic caution to them and check potassium if kidney function is reduced.

### Report rank 2: Kaged — Kaged Citrulline (fermented L-citrulline powder, unflavored, 200 g)

Two or more verification batches checked this product independently; all 2 records are kept so their differences stay visible.

#### Verification record 1 of 2

- **Verifier verdict:** recommend — A directly confirmed, worldwide Informed Sport listing (certified since 2017, batches tested through June 2026) for a single fermented L-citrulline with no malate at the 1.5-2 g dose used in the ED study, best taken as the citrulline half of a DIY Prelox with Pycnogenol because citrulline alone rests on one small study.
- **Product as verified:** Kaged — Kaged Citrulline (fermented L-citrulline powder, unflavored, 200 g)
- **Product page:** https://www.kaged.com/products/citrulline
- **Where sold:** US/Canada (kaged.com, ca.kaged.com, Vitacost). Informed Sport lists regional availability in Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, Oceania and South America. Gulf: Sporter storefronts for Saudi Arabia and Kuwait (in stock when crawled), Qatar, and Bahrain (out of stock); stock varies. The separate Kaged citrulline capsule product was not checked for certification.
- **Label verified:** yes. Kaged's own US and Canadian product pages and FAQ (read through the search index of kaged.com), consistent with the Sporter Bahrain listing (2 g scoop, about 100 servings). kaged.com could not be opened directly this session.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving size 1 rounded scoop (2 g): L-citrulline 2,000 mg as AMINATURE, a patented fermented, plant-based (corn-sourced) free-form L-citrulline with no malate; about 100 servings per 200 g; unflavored, no other actives. Directions: start with 1 scoop 20-30 minutes before training; many athletes use 2 scoops (4 g). For a Prelox replacement: 3/4 scoop (1.5 g, the ED-trial dose) to 1 scoop (2 g) once daily.
- **Dose versus trials (matches):** 3/4 scoop = 1.5 g, the dose of the only citrulline-alone ED study (Cormio 2011: 1.5 g/day for 1 month after a 1-month placebo phase, single-blind, 24 men aged about 56 with mild ED; 12/24 vs 2/24 reached normal erection hardness; intercourse 1.37 to 2.3 per month; no adverse events) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/]. One scoop (2 g) is slightly above it. Shirai 2018 used only 800 mg citrulline + 300 mg resveratrol as an add-on to PDE5 inhibitors [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6302103/]. Pharmacokinetics: citrulline raised plasma arginine more than arginine itself, dose-dependently up to 3 g twice daily, in a 20-volunteer double-blind crossover [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17662090/]. Because there is no malate, the gram figure is real citrulline. Against Prelox: no Pycnogenol; the Prelox-family product that used citrulline (Prelox R) gave 1.2 g citrulline + 1.92 g arginine + 80 mg Pycnogenol + 40 mg roburins a day [https://www.oatext.com/review-influence-of-combinations-of-pycnogenolr-and-l-arginine-aspartate-on-mens-virility-and-fertility.php].
- **Certification:** Informed Sport. Listing 'Citrulline' (KAGED), read directly from the certifier's database on 8 Nov 2026: 'Certified Product' with no withdrawal notice; date certified 22-Aug-2017; unflavored powder; regions Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, Oceania, South America; 13 batch rows, latest 22561 tested 24-Jun-2026 (expires 30-Jun-2028), 22748 tested 19-Jun-2026, 22404 tested 08-Jun-2026, 21993 tested 23-Apr-2026, back to 25411 tested 13-May-2025. A third-party aggregator (SupplementChecker) and one search summary wrongly say Kaged is not Informed Sport tested; the database page contradicts them. Informed Sport tests every batch before release for more than 285 substances banned in sport using ISO 17025 methods [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice]; it is a drug-screening programme, but whether its panel includes PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil-type drugs) was not confirmed, whereas BSCG's published menu names tadalafil, vardenafil and many sildenafil analogues [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725]. Certification is lot-specific: match the batch number on the container to the database. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/kaged/citrulline; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Kaged (also sold as Kaged Muscle), US sports-nutrition brand. None found: a web search for 'Kaged recall OR FDA warning letter' (8 Nov 2026) returned only other companies' FDA warning letters and recalls (Muscle Sports Products, Chaotic Labz and others), nothing naming Kaged. A third-party aggregator reports no FDA recalls and 1 adverse-event report for the whole brand (not product-specific, unverified) [https://supplementchecker.co/brand/kaged-muscle/]. Not searched: FDA's warning-letter and recall databases directly, Health Canada, TGA, MHRA. Live re-checks were impossible in this pass (WebSearch budget exhausted; fda.gov, sport.wetestyoutrust.com, nowfoods.com, kaged.com, DSLD and Europe PMC blocked; Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC), so these findings rest on the workflow's 8 Nov 2026 discovery reads and the project's earlier notes.
- **Evidence summary:** Citrulline-alone ED evidence is thin: one single-blind, non-randomized-order pilot of 24 men (Cormio 2011) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/], never replicated; a 2024 network meta-analysis of 15 placebo-controlled RCTs (1,000 men) found arginine, not citrulline, effective among single nutraceuticals [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/39279185]; Cleveland Clinic says 'the jury's still out' on citrulline for ED [https://health.clevelandclinic.org/citrulline-benefits]; ConsumerLab notes modest to no benefit in exercise and cardiovascular studies [https://www.consumerlab.com/reviews/l-citrulline-supplements/l-citrulline/]. Mechanistic support is good: it bypasses intestinal arginase and raises plasma arginine and NO markers more than oral arginine [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17662090/]. Best used as the amino-acid half of a Prelox-type regimen with Pycnogenol.

**Safety risks**

- Lowers blood pressure; adds to blood-pressure drugs and some ED medicines, and is risky with nitrates [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794; https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- Gastrointestinal upset: bloating, cramping, diarrhea, sweating [https://health.clevelandclinic.org/citrulline-benefits]; stomach discomfort in 6 of 41 weight lifters [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/]; ConsumerLab notes occasional GI upset and heartburn [https://www.consumerlab.com/reviews/l-citrulline-supplements/l-citrulline/].
- Long-term safety not adequately assessed for months-long use; short-term studies up to 6 g/day for 4 weeks found no adverse effects [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/]; single doses of 2-15 g caused no side effects in 8 healthy men [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17953788/].
- Serious allergic reactions are possible but uncommon [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- No stimulants, hormones or botanicals; single amino acid not marketed for sexual enhancement.

**Interactions**

- Nitrates (nitroglycerin, isosorbide): avoid; additive blood-pressure fall [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline]; Drugs.com: L-arginine or L-citrulline may potentiate nitrates and vasodilators [https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine-with-sildenafil-232-0-2061-0.html?professional=1].
- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil, vardenafil, avanafil): listed as interacting (additive blood-pressure lowering) [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline; https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794]; a small crossover adding 800 mg citrulline + 300 mg resveratrol to PDE5i users reported no adverse events [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6302103/].
- Antihypertensives: blood pressure may go too low [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794; https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- Surgery: stop at least 2 weeks before scheduled surgery [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].
- Anticoagulants/antiplatelets, diabetes drugs, thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction listed in the WebMD or Natural Medicines summaries reviewed [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline; https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men who take nitrates [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- Men with low blood pressure, or on several blood-pressure drugs or a PDE5 inhibitor, unless a clinician supervises [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].
- Men with a past heart attack, heart or blood-vessel disease, or kidney or liver problems should clear it with a clinician first [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline]; because citrulline raises plasma arginine more than arginine itself [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17662090/], the post-heart-attack arginine caution [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/] is prudent here too (inference).
- Anyone with surgery scheduled within 2 weeks [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].
- Men with new, unexplained ED before a cardiovascular check [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16414947/].

**Stop signals**

- Dizziness, light-headedness or fainting (blood pressure too low).
- Chest pain, breathlessness or new palpitations: stop and seek emergency care.
- Hives, swelling of face/lips/throat, wheezing or racing heart: stop and get help [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- Persistent bloating, cramping, diarrhea or heartburn [https://health.clevelandclinic.org/citrulline-benefits].
- Being prescribed nitrates or a new blood-pressure drug, or surgery being scheduled: stop and review [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].
- No noticeable benefit after 1-3 months (the ED study lasted 1 month) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/].

Its 50% vs 8.3% response therefore compares consecutive months, not two groups, and it has never been replicated. The 2024 network meta-analysis found arginine effective, not citrulline.

#### Verification record 2 of 2

- **Verifier verdict:** recommend — A directly confirmed, worldwide Informed Sport listing (certified since 2017, batches tested through June 2026) for a single fermented L-citrulline with no malate, at the 1.5-2 g dose used in the ED study, makes this a top certified vascular pick, best taken as the citrulline half of a DIY Prelox with Pycnogenol because citrulline alone rests on one small study.
- **Product as verified:** Kaged — Kaged Citrulline (fermented L-citrulline powder, unflavored, 200 g)
- **Product page:** https://www.kaged.com/products/citrulline
- **Where sold:** US and Canada: kaged.com, the Canadian Kaged site and Vitacost. Informed Sport lists availability in Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, Oceania and South America. Gulf: Sporter storefronts for Saudi Arabia and Kuwait (in stock when crawled), Qatar and Bahrain (Bahrain out of stock); no UAE listing was found. UK/EU retailer not verified.
- **Label verified:** yes. Kaged's US and Canadian product pages and FAQ, read via the search index of kaged.com on 8 Nov 2026 (https://www.kaged.com/products/citrulline; https://www.kaged.com/en-ca/products/citrulline), consistent with the Sporter Bahrain listing (2 g scoop, about 100 servings; https://www.sporter.com/en-bh/kaged-muscle-citrulline/). kaged.com could not be opened directly.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving 1 rounded scoop (2 g): L-citrulline 2,000 mg as AMINATURE, a patented fermented, plant-based (corn-sourced) free-form L-citrulline with no malate. About 100 servings per 200 g; unflavoured, no other actives. Kaged's directions are training-oriented: start with 1 scoop 20-30 minutes before training, and many athletes use 2 scoops (4 g). As a Prelox-type vascular replacement, take 3/4 scoop (1.5 g, the ED-trial dose) to 1 scoop (2 g) once daily. A separate Kaged capsule product exists (2 capsules per serving); its certification was not checked.
- **Dose versus trials (matches):** Three-quarters of a scoop gives 1.5 g, the dose in the only citrulline-alone ED study. Cormio 2011 gave 1.5 g/day for 1 month after a 1-month placebo phase (single-blind, 24 men averaging about 56 years with mild ED). 12/24 versus 2/24 reached normal erection hardness, intercourse episodes rose from 1.37 to 2.3 a month, and there were no adverse events (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/). One scoop (2 g) is slightly above that. Shirai 2018 used only 800 mg citrulline + 300 mg resveratrol as an add-on to PDE5 inhibitors (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6302103/). Citrulline raised plasma arginine more than arginine itself, dose-dependently up to 3 g twice daily (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17662090/). With no malate, the gram figure is real citrulline. There is no Pycnogenol; Horphag's Prelox R gave 1.2 g citrulline + 1.92 g arginine + 80 mg Pycnogenol + 40 mg roburins a day.
- **Certification:** Informed Sport. The 'Citrulline' (KAGED) listing was read directly from the certifier's database in this workflow on 8 Nov 2026 (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/kaged/citrulline). It shows 'Certified Product' with no withdrawal notice; certified 22-Aug-2017; powder; regions Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, Oceania, South America. There are 13 unflavoured batch rows. The latest is 22561, tested 24-Jun-2026 (expires 30-Jun-2028); then 22748 (19-Jun-2026), 22404 (08-Jun-2026), 21993 (23-Apr-2026), 27072 (27-Mar-2026) and 21797 (17-Mar-2026), back to 25411 (13-May-2025). One discovery search summary and an aggregator (SupplementChecker) wrongly said it was no longer, or not, Informed Sport tested; the database page contradicts them. Informed Sport tests every batch for more than 285 substances banned in sport (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice). It does not publish whether PDE5 inhibitors are on its panel; BSCG's menu names them. Match the tub's batch number to the listing. For context, PODIUM L-Citrulline was voluntarily withdrawn from Informed Sport on 29-Sep-2026 (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/podium-nutrition/podium-l-citrulline). (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/kaged/citrulline; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Kaged (also sold as Kaged Muscle) is a US sports-nutrition brand. A web search on 8 Nov 2026 for 'Kaged recall OR FDA warning letter Kaged Muscle supplement' returned only other companies' FDA actions (Muscle Sports Products, Chaotic Labz and others), nothing naming Kaged. An aggregator reports no FDA recalls and 1 adverse-event report for the whole brand, unverified and not product-specific (https://supplementchecker.co/brand/kaged-muscle/). Not searched: FDA's warning-letter and recall databases directly, Health Canada, TGA, MHRA. A live re-check was impossible this pass (WebSearch limit used up, WebFetch blocked, Firecrawl unavailable). No red flag is known.
- **Evidence summary:** Citrulline-alone ED evidence is thin: one single-blind, non-randomised-order pilot of 24 men (Cormio 2011), never replicated. A 2024 network meta-analysis of 15 placebo-controlled RCTs found arginine, not citrulline, effective among single nutraceuticals (https://europepmc.org/article/MED/39279185). Cleveland Clinic says 'the jury's still out' on citrulline for ED, and ConsumerLab notes modest to no benefit in exercise and cardiovascular studies (https://www.consumerlab.com/reviews/l-citrulline-supplements/l-citrulline/). The mechanism is well supported: citrulline bypasses intestinal arginase and raises plasma arginine more than oral arginine. It is best used as the amino-acid half of a Prelox-type regimen with Pycnogenol.

**Safety risks**

- Lowers blood pressure; adds to blood-pressure drugs and some ED medicines, and is risky with nitrates (Natural Medicines; WebMD).
- GI upset (bloating, cramping, diarrhoea, sweating) (Cleveland Clinic); stomach discomfort in 6 of 41 weight lifters (ODS); occasional heartburn (ConsumerLab).
- Long-term safety has not been assessed for months-long use. Up to 6 g/day for 4 weeks caused no adverse effects (ODS), and single doses of 2-15 g caused none in healthy men (Moinard 2008).
- Serious allergic reactions are possible but uncommon (WebMD).
- Because citrulline raises plasma arginine, the post-heart-attack arginine caution (VINTAGE MI) is prudent too (inference).
- Category quality risk: some citrulline products contained less than claimed or none (Examine); batch-tested certification reduces this for Kaged.
- No stimulants, hormones or botanicals; not marketed for sexual enhancement.

**Interactions**

- Nitrates (nitroglycerin, isosorbide, nicorandil, 'poppers'): avoid; additive blood-pressure fall (WebMD; Drugs.com).
- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil, vardenafil, avanafil): additive blood-pressure lowering (WebMD; Natural Medicines); 800 mg citrulline + resveratrol added to PDE5i users caused no adverse events (Shirai 2018).
- Antihypertensives and alpha-blockers: blood pressure may go too low (Natural Medicines; Drugs.com).
- Surgery: stop at least 2 weeks before (Natural Medicines).
- Anticoagulants/antiplatelets, diabetes drugs, thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: none listed for citrulline (WebMD; Natural Medicines); arginine's bleeding and glucose cautions apply only in theory (Mayo Clinic).

**Who should avoid it**

- Men who take nitrates or use 'poppers'.
- Men with low blood pressure, or on several blood-pressure drugs or a PDE5 inhibitor, unless a clinician supervises.
- Men with a past heart attack, heart or blood-vessel disease, or kidney or liver problems should clear it with a clinician first (WebMD).
- Anyone with surgery scheduled within 2 weeks.
- Men with new, unexplained ED who have not had a cardiovascular check (AUA).

**Stop signals**

- Dizziness, light-headedness or fainting.
- Chest pain, breathlessness or new palpitations: seek emergency care.
- Hives, facial or throat swelling, wheezing or a racing heart (WebMD).
- Persistent bloating, cramping, diarrhoea or heartburn.
- Being prescribed nitrates or a new blood-pressure drug, or having surgery scheduled: stop and review.
- An erection lasting more than 4 hours: emergency care.
- No noticeable benefit after 1-3 months (Rosen 2011 minimal clinically important difference).

### Report rank 3: NOW Foods (NOW Sports) — NOW Sports Arginine & Citrulline Powder, unflavored, 12 oz (340 g)

Two or more verification batches checked this product independently; all 2 records are kept so their differences stay visible.

#### Verification record 1 of 2

- **Verifier verdict:** recommend — Two fully disclosed amino acids with nothing else, an Informed Sport listing with batches tested from 2024 to March 2026, and one level teaspoon gives the trial citrulline dose plus Prelox-level arginine, so adding 80 mg Pycnogenol rebuilds a Prelox-type regimen with every milligram known.
- **Product as verified:** NOW Foods (NOW Sports) — NOW Sports Arginine & Citrulline Powder, unflavored, 12 oz (340 g)
- **Product page:** https://www.vitacost.com/products/now-foods-sports-arginine-citrulline-powder-12-oz-340-g-85183
- **Where sold:** US (Vitacost); UK (Welzo); Saudi Arabia (Nahdi, 340 g tub). No UAE, Sporter or iHerb-Gulf listing was found. Do not confuse with NOW's Arginine & Citrulline 500 mg/250 mg capsules (also sold at Nahdi), which are a different product and not the Informed Sport-listed item.
- **Label verified:** yes. Supplement Facts as reproduced by Vitacost, Professional Supplement Center and Nahdi, which agree (1 level teaspoon about 3 g; 1.5 g + 1.5 g; about 113 servings). One reseller (myGP) shows a conflicting 10 g serving with 5 g + 5 g and 34 servings, treated as a listing error. NOW's own page and NIH DSLD could not be opened in this session.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving size 1 level teaspoon (about 3 g), about 113 servings per 340 g tub. Per serving: L-arginine (free-form) 1.5 g; L-citrulline (free-form) 1.5 g; 15 kcal; unflavored, no other ingredients shown on the retailer copy. The label's own suggested-use wording was not captured (the conflicting myGP page says one ~10 g scoop once daily; follow the tub). For a Prelox replacement: 1 level teaspoon daily (1.5 g + 1.5 g), up to 2 teaspoons (3 g + 3 g) to reach the arginine doses of the positive trials; take with water, split if GI upset.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** L-citrulline: 1 tsp = 1.5 g, exactly the dose of the only citrulline-alone ED study (Cormio 2011: 1.5 g/day for 1 month, single-blind, 24 men with mild ED; erection hardness improved from 3 to 4 in 50% vs 8.3% on placebo) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/]. L-arginine: 1 tsp = 1.5 g, the dose that was no better than placebo when used alone (Klotz 1999, 17 days) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10743698/]; 2 tsp = 3 g, the dose combined with sildenafil in El-Wakeel 2020 (8 weeks) [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31267684] and just above Gallo 2020's 2.5 g/day (IIEF-EF +3.1 overall, +5.4 in mild ED, 12 weeks, not placebo-controlled) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/]; meta-analysis range 1.5-5 g/day [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/30770070]. Against Prelox: the Prelox trials gave 80 mg Pycnogenol + 2.8-3 g L-arginine aspartate a day [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/], which by molecular weight is only about 1.6-1.7 g of arginine (my calculation), so 1 tsp already supplies Prelox-level arginine plus 1.5 g citrulline. It closely mirrors the amino-acid part of Horphag's Prelox R (80 mg Pycnogenol + 1.92 g arginine + 1.2 g citrulline + 40 mg roburins/day, 50-man double-blind crossover, erectile function 'restored to normal' in 1 month) [https://www.oatext.com/review-influence-of-combinations-of-pycnogenolr-and-l-arginine-aspartate-on-mens-virility-and-fertility.php; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/25664962], but it contains no Pycnogenol, which has to be added separately.
- **Certification:** Informed Sport. Listing 'NOW Sports Arginine & Citrulline' (powder): date certified 23-Jul-2018; batch 3450953 (unflavored, expires 31-Mar-2028) tested 26-Mar-2026, with earlier tests on 7-Nov-2025, 1-Aug-2025, 11-Mar-2025 and 11-Dec-2024. Seen through the search-engine index of the certifier's own database page on 8 Nov 2026 (three consistent searches); the live page could not be reopened this session. The product is also named on Informed Sport's November-2026 certified-products list. Informed Sport tests every batch before release for more than 285 substances banned in sport using ISO 17025 methods [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice]; it is a drug-screening programme, but whether its panel includes PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil-type drugs) was not confirmed, whereas BSCG's published menu names tadalafil, vardenafil and many sildenafil analogues [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725]. Certification is lot-specific: match the batch number on the container to the database. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/now-foods/now-sports-arginine-citrulline; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** NOW Foods / NOW Health Group (US manufacturer). Found: a 2005 FDA warning letter over possible quintozene (pesticide) contamination of one lot of American ginseng [https://www.supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/now-responds-to-fda-warning-letter]; a 2016 voluntary recall of six unrelated supplements (about 165 units) after a label-printer error [https://www.Drugs.com/fda/now-health-group-dietary-supplements-recall-mislabeling-13846.html]; an earlier voluntary recall of a phosphatidylserine product that did not meet its label claim [https://wholefoodsmagazine.com/keywords/12602-recall]; and, per a recall aggregator, 2025 food recalls of NOW Nutritional Yeast Powder (Class III, May 2025, terminated) and organic raw cashews (Class II, Sept 2025) [https://recallcheck.net/brand/now-foods; https://recallcheck.net/food-recalls/5197]. None found for any NOW arginine or citrulline product, and the searches surfaced no FDA hidden-drug (tainted product) notice for NOW, although FDA's tainted-products database itself was not queried. Searched: web search 'NOW Foods recall OR warning letter FDA 2024 2025 2026 supplement' (8 Nov 2026). Not searched: FDA's warning-letter and enforcement-report databases directly, Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. Live re-checks were impossible in this pass (WebSearch budget exhausted; fda.gov, sport.wetestyoutrust.com, nowfoods.com, kaged.com, DSLD and Europe PMC blocked; Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC), so these findings rest on the workflow's 8 Nov 2026 discovery reads and the project's earlier notes.
- **Evidence summary:** Arginine has the stronger ED evidence: meta-analysis of 10 RCTs (540 men) OR 3.37 for improvement at 1.5-5 g/day [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/30770070]; a placebo-controlled 6 g/day RCT in vasculogenic ED [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3]; the only single nutraceutical with significant efficacy in a 2024 network meta-analysis [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/39279185]; EAU weak recommendation for mild ED with counselling that the gain may be small [https://uroweb.org/guidelines/sexual-and-reproductive-health/chapter/management-of-erectile-dysfunction]. Citrulline alone rests on one 24-man single-blind pilot [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/] but raises plasma arginine more effectively than arginine itself [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17662090/]. No trial has tested this arginine + citrulline pairing without Pycnogenol; the closest is Prelox R, which included both amino acids plus Pycnogenol and roburins [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/25664962].

**Safety risks**

- Post-heart-attack mortality signal: in VINTAGE MI (L-arginine 3 g three times daily = 9 g/day for 6 months after a first STEMI) 6 arginine patients (8.6%) died vs none on placebo and enrolment was closed; authors: 'L-arginine should not be recommended following acute myocardial infarction' [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/]. Mayo and Cleveland Clinic both say not to use after a recent heart attack [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Lowers blood pressure; with nitrates the drop can be dangerous, and it adds to blood-pressure drugs and PDE5 inhibitors [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Gastrointestinal effects (nausea, abdominal pain, diarrhea, bloating) [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; single doses of 3-6 g rarely provoke them, most occur at single doses above 9 g and divided doses help [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17513449/].
- Other reported effects: gout, headache, allergic reactions, airway inflammation or worse asthma [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; insomnia in 5% at 2.5 g/day [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/].
- Long-term safety: NIH ODS says up to 9 g/day is safe and well tolerated for several days or weeks, with GI upset and slightly lower blood pressure at 9-30 g/day [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/]; studies have lasted up to about 3 months [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-Consumer/].
- High potassium if combined with potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Lowers blood pressure; adds to blood-pressure drugs and some ED medicines, and is risky with nitrates [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794; https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- Gastrointestinal upset: bloating, cramping, diarrhea, sweating [https://health.clevelandclinic.org/citrulline-benefits]; stomach discomfort in 6 of 41 weight lifters [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/]; ConsumerLab notes occasional GI upset and heartburn [https://www.consumerlab.com/reviews/l-citrulline-supplements/l-citrulline/].
- Long-term safety not adequately assessed for months-long use; short-term studies up to 6 g/day for 4 weeks found no adverse effects [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/]; single doses of 2-15 g caused no side effects in 8 healthy men [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17953788/].
- Serious allergic reactions are possible but uncommon [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- Teaspoon dosing is approximate and one reseller lists a 10 g serving; use the tub's own Supplement Facts.
- No stimulants, hormones or botanicals; it is not marketed as a sexual-enhancement product, the category where FDA's hidden-drug notices cluster [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications].

**Interactions**

- Nitrates (nitroglycerin, isosorbide mononitrate/dinitrate): do not combine; blood pressure may fall too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine]; Drugs.com: arginine may potentiate nitrates and other NO donors [https://www.drugs.com/npp/l-arginine.html].
- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil): Mayo warns sildenafil plus L-arginine may drop blood pressure too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; Drugs.com rates arginine + sildenafil as a moderate interaction and advises monitoring blood pressure and heart rate [https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine-with-sildenafil-232-0-2061-0.html?professional=1]; deliberate combination trials found more dyspepsia/gastritis but no significant rise in adverse events overall [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31267684; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/33587304].
- Antihypertensives (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, beta-blockers, calcium-channel blockers, alpha-blockers such as doxazosin and prazosin; tamsulosin rated moderate by Drugs.com): additive blood-pressure lowering [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/l-arginine-with-tamsulosin-232-4042-2146-0.html?professional=1].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (e.g., warfarin, aspirin): may increase bleeding risk [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Diabetes drugs: L-arginine may lower blood sugar so doses may need adjusting [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; effect with insulin is unpredictable [https://www.drugs.com/npp/l-arginine.html].
- Potassium-sparing diuretics (amiloride, spironolactone, triamterene): do not combine, risk of hyperkalemia [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Isoproterenol: blood pressure may fall too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction is listed by Mayo Clinic or Cleveland Clinic [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Surgery: stop at least 2 weeks before scheduled surgery [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone with a recent heart attack (VINTAGE MI; Mayo; Cleveland Clinic) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with coronary artery disease or heart failure unless a cardiologist approves (NOW's Arabic L-arginine tablet page on Nahdi says not to use with coronary artery disease or after a heart attack; Cleveland Clinic lists heart failure as a serious reaction) [https://www.nahdionline.com/ar-sa/now-foods-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets/pdp/101506676; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Men who take nitrates, or potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with low blood pressure, or on several blood-pressure drugs or a PDE5 inhibitor, unless a clinician supervises [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- People with asthma or significant allergies (may worsen) [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- People with recurrent herpes simplex or glaucoma should ask a physician first (caution printed on NOW's Canadian L-arginine tablet label) [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets].
- Guanidinoacetate methyltransferase deficiency; adults over 65 without provider approval [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Men with new, unexplained ED who have not had a cardiovascular check, because ED predicts cardiovascular events [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16414947/].
- Anyone with surgery scheduled within 2 weeks [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].

**Stop signals**

- Dizziness, light-headedness or fainting (blood pressure too low).
- Chest pain, breathlessness or new palpitations: stop and seek emergency care.
- Hives, facial or throat swelling, or wheezing: stop and seek urgent care [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Persistent diarrhea, nausea, bloating or heartburn; a gout flare; worsening asthma [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- A cold-sore (herpes) outbreak, given the herpes caution on NOW's Canadian arginine label [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets].
- Being prescribed nitrates, a potassium-sparing diuretic or a new blood-pressure drug: stop and review with the prescriber.
- No noticeable benefit after about 3 months (the length of the longest arginine-alone trials) [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3].
- Surgery being scheduled: stop at least 2 weeks before [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - The listing itself was never opened in this workflow: the batch data (certified 23-Jul-2018; batch 3450953 tested 26-Mar-2026, plus tests on 7-Nov-2025, 1-Aug-2025, 11-Mar-2025 and 11-Dec-2024) come only from search-engine summaries of https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/now-foods/now-sports-arginine-citrulline. Those summaries are not reliable on their own: the same kind of summary called Kaged Citrulline 'no longer certified', while the directly captured Kaged page shows it certified (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/kaged/citrulline).
  - Independent corroboration I found: Informed Sport's own generated 'CERTIFIED PRODUCTS November-2026' list (PDF versions dated 30 Oct and 3 Nov 2026) names 'NOW Sports Arginine & Citrulline' (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/sites/default/files/generated-pdf/file.pdf), and the directly captured NOW L-Arginine tablet listing (8 Nov 2026) links to this listing with the image label 'NOW Sports Arginine & Citrulline Powder - Bottle', so the listed form is the powder (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/now-foods/l-arginine-1000mg-tabs).
  - No batch tested after 26-Mar-2026 is visible in the indexed copy, whereas NOW's tablet listing shows tests on 2 and 9 Oct 2026; match the tub's batch number on the live page.
  - Label: NOW's own panel and directions were never read (nowfoods.com and DSLD blocked). The conflicting myGP panel (10 g serving = 5 g + 5 g, 34 servings) is internally consistent (34 x 10 g = 340 g), so it may be a real label variant rather than a typo (https://store.mygp.com/products/arginine-and-citrulline-powder-now-foods-340-grams). The 1:1 ratio is the same in both versions.
  - No reformulation evidence found.

- *safety-evidence lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*
  - Certification only checked indirectly: batch 3450953 (tested 26-Mar-2026) came from a search-index copy, not the live listing. PODIUM L-Citrulline's indexed copy still said 'certified' after it was withdrawn on 29-Sep-2026, so certification_verified should read 'indirect' until the tub's batch ID is matched on the live page. NOW's tablet listing, which was read live, does show NOW is still active in the programme.
  - Hidden-drug screening is overstated. Informed Sport tests for substances banned in sport. PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil and their analogues, the main hidden drugs in sexual-enhancement supplements) are not on WADA's Prohibited List, and BSCG lists them as non-sport drugs it adds on top of sport panels. Read screens_for_hidden_drugs as 'sport-banned drugs only; PDE5 coverage unconfirmed'. The practical risk is low for a plain amino-acid powder.
  - The evidence is framed too strongly. Taking this powder with Pycnogenol bought separately is an untested do-it-yourself regimen, not a rebuilt Prelox: the Prelox RCTs used arginine aspartate with no citrulline, and Prelox R (the version with citrulline) also contained roburins. Cormio 2011 ran in a fixed order (a placebo month, then a citrulline month, single-blind, not randomised). Rhim 2019's OR 3.37 pools arginine-only trials with combination products (Prelox, arginine plus yohimbine).
  - Missed interaction (inference): ACE inhibitors and ARBs, the most common blood-pressure drugs in men in their 40s, also raise potassium. Mayo names only potassium-sparing diuretics, but a man on an ACE inhibitor or ARB, especially with reduced kidney function, should have his potassium checked after starting multi-gram arginine. Drugs.com lists electrolyte imbalance as arginine's disease interaction.
  - Record details: the NOW ginseng (quintozene) warning letter dates from October 2004 according to trade press at the time, not 2005, and the 2016 label-error recall was published as a soy-allergy alert. Nothing was found on any NOW arginine or citrulline product. FDA, MHRA and Health Canada databases could not be re-queried this pass because the search limit was used up and those hosts are blocked.

#### Verification record 2 of 2

- **Verifier verdict:** recommend — It has two fully disclosed amino acids and nothing else, an Informed Sport listing with batches tested through March 2026 from a maker still active in the programme, and one level teaspoon gives the trial citrulline dose plus Prelox-level arginine. Confirm the 3 g serving on the tub, and add 80 mg Pycnogenol separately to rebuild the full Prelox regimen.
- **Product as verified:** NOW Foods (NOW Sports) — NOW Sports Arginine & Citrulline Powder, unflavored, 12 oz (340 g). This powder is the form Informed Sport lists; the 500 mg/250 mg Arginine & Citrulline veg capsules are a different product.
- **Product page:** https://www.vitacost.com/products/now-foods-sports-arginine-citrulline-powder-12-oz-340-g-85183
- **Where sold:** US (Vitacost); UK (Welzo); Saudi Arabia (Nahdi, 340 g tub); Canada (a Well.ca listing named in discovery, form not re-checked). No UAE, Qatar or other GCC listing found. The 120-veg-capsule product (500 mg arginine + 250 mg citrulline per capsule; sold at HEB, Vitasave, Nahdi and others) is a different product, and no Informed Sport listing was found for it.
- **Label verified:** yes. Supplement Facts as reproduced by three retailers (Vitacost, Professional Supplement Center, Nahdi), read through search-engine summaries on 8 Nov 2026. All three agree. One reseller (myGP) prints a conflicting serving (10 g, giving 5 g + 5 g, 34 servings), treated here as a listing error. NOW's own page and NIH DSLD could not be reached this session, so check the tub.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving size: 1 level teaspoon (approx. 3 g); about 113 servings per 340 g tub. Per serving: L-arginine (free-form) 1.5 g and L-citrulline (free-form) 1.5 g; 15 kcal; unflavored, no other ingredients shown. The tub's own suggested-use wording was not captured (the conflicting myGP page says one ~10 g scoop once daily, before or after exercise). As a Prelox replacement: 1 level teaspoon a day gives 1.5 g arginine + 1.5 g citrulline. Up to 2 teaspoons a day, split morning and evening, gives 3 g + 3 g. Contains no Pycnogenol.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** L-citrulline: 1 tsp = 1.5 g/day. That is exactly the dose of the only citrulline-alone ED study (Cormio 2011: 1.5 g/day for 1 month, single-blind, 24 men with mild ED; erection hardness rose from 3 to 4 in 50% vs 8.3% on placebo) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/].
L-arginine: 1 tsp = 1.5 g/day, a dose that was no better than placebo over 17 days (Klotz 1999) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10743698/]. 2 tsp = 3 g/day, which is:
- inside the 1.5-5 g/day range of the Rhim 2019 meta-analysis (10 RCTs, 540 men, OR 3.37 for improvement) [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/30770070];
- close to Gallo 2020 (2.5 g/day) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/] and El-Wakeel 2020 (3 g/day) [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31267684];
- half of Menafra 2022 (6 g/day, 98 men, 3 months) [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3].
Compared with Prelox: the Prelox trials gave 80 mg Pycnogenol + 2.8-3 g L-arginine aspartate a day (Ledda 2010; Stanislavov 2008) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/]. Stanislavov 2003 equates 3 g arginine aspartate to about 1.7 g L-arginine [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/12851125]. So 1 tsp already gives Prelox-level arginine plus 1.5 g citrulline. A Horphag-linked 2018 paper describes a later 'Prelox R' regimen of 80 mg Pycnogenol + 1.92 g arginine + 1.2 g citrulline + 40 mg roburins a day [https://esmed.org/MRA/mra/article/download/1865/1917]. This powder has no Pycnogenol, so the Prelox regimen is rebuilt only if 80 mg Pycnogenol is added separately.
- **Certification:** Informed Sport tests every batch before release for more than 285 substances banned in sport, using ISO 17025 methods [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice].
Listing 'NOW Sports Arginine & Citrulline' (powder):
- date certified 23-Jul-2018;
- batch 3450953 (unflavoured, expires 31-Mar-2028) tested 26-Mar-2026;
- earlier tests 7-Nov-2025, 1-Aug-2025, 11-Mar-2025 and 11-Dec-2024.
These details came from the search-engine index of the certifier's own database page on 8 Nov 2026 (consistent across searches); the live page could not be opened. Indexed copies can lag: PODIUM L-Citrulline's indexed copy still said 'certified' while its live page shows it was withdrawn. However, NOW's sibling listing 'L-ARGININE 1000mg tabs', read directly on 8 Nov 2026, shows a batch tested on 9-Oct-2026 [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/now-foods/l-arginine-1000mg-tabs], so NOW is still active in the programme.
Informed Sport's panel is built around substances banned in sport. Whether it covers sildenafil-type PDE5 inhibitors was not confirmed; BSCG's published menu does name them [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725]. Match the batch number on the tub to the database before relying on it. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/now-foods/now-sports-arginine-citrulline; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: NOW Foods / NOW Health Group (US).
Found:
- An FDA warning letter about possible quintozene (pesticide) contamination of one lot of NOW American ginseng; NOW then changed its ginseng testing. Trade-press dates put the letter in October 2004; one search summary says 2005 [https://www.supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/now-responds-to-fda-warning-letter; https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2004/11/18/NOW-Foods-makes-ginseng-changes-after-FDA-letter/].
- A 2016 voluntary recall of six unrelated supplements (about 165 units) after a label supplier's printing error [https://www.Drugs.com/fda/now-health-group-dietary-supplements-recall-mislabeling-13846.html].
- An earlier voluntary recall of a phosphatidylserine product that did not meet its label claim [https://wholefoodsmagazine.com/keywords/12602-recall].
- Food recalls of NOW Nutritional Yeast Powder (Class III, May 2025, terminated) and organic raw cashews (Class II, Sept 2025) [https://recallcheck.net/food-recalls/5197; https://recallcheck.net/brand/now-foods].
Not found: anything on a NOW arginine or citrulline product, any FDA warning letter from 2024-2026, or any FDA hidden-drug notice naming NOW.
Searched: the workflow's 8 Nov 2026 web search 'NOW Foods recall OR warning letter FDA 2024 2025 2026 supplement'.
Not searched: FDA's warning-letter, enforcement-report and tainted-product databases directly, Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. This session's web-search budget was used up, the network policy blocked regulator hosts, and Firecrawl credits ran out until 9 Nov 2026 05:26 UTC.
- **Evidence summary:** Arginine is the better-evidenced half of this product:
- Rhim 2019 meta-analysis (10 RCTs, 540 men, mild-moderate ED): 1.5-5 g/day improved ED (OR 3.37), with mild adverse effects in 8.3% vs 2.3% on placebo [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/30770070].
- Menafra 2022 (placebo-controlled, 6 g/day): IIEF-6 median rose from 20 to 24 over 3 months [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3].
- The EAU gives a weak recommendation for daily arginine (or ginseng) in mild ED, with counselling that the gain may be small [https://uroweb.org/guidelines/sexual-and-reproductive-health/chapter/management-of-erectile-dysfunction].
Citrulline alone rests on one 24-man single-blind pilot [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/], although oral citrulline raises plasma arginine more effectively than oral arginine does [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC2291275/].
No trial has tested arginine + citrulline without Pycnogenol. The nearest is Horphag's Prelox R combination (Pycnogenol + roburins + arginine + citrulline): in a 50-man, 1-month double-blind crossover it 'restored erectile function to normal' [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/25664962].

**Safety risks**

- Post-heart-attack mortality signal: in VINTAGE MI (L-arginine about 3 g three times daily for 6 months after a first STEMI), 6 arginine patients (8.6%) died vs none on placebo, and enrolment was stopped. The authors say arginine should not be recommended after an acute MI [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/; https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jama/fullarticle/202136].
- Lowers blood pressure. The drop can be dangerous with nitrates, and it adds to blood-pressure drugs and PDE5 inhibitors; Mayo Clinic says not to take L-arginine with sildenafil [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394].
- Gastrointestinal upset (nausea, cramps, diarrhoea, bloating). Single doses of 3-6 g rarely cause it; most occurs above 9 g per dose, and divided doses help [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17513449/; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]. Citrulline caused stomach discomfort in 6 of 41 weight lifters [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/].
- Other reported effects of arginine: gout, headache, allergic reactions, airway inflammation or worse asthma [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; insomnia in 5% at 2.5 g/day in a 12-week trial [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/].
- High blood potassium if combined with potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Long-term data are limited. NIH ODS says up to 9 g/day arginine is safe for days to weeks, that safety beyond 3 months is not known, and that citrulline's safety over months of use has not been adequately assessed [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/].
- Teaspoon dosing is approximate, and one reseller lists a 10 g serving, so follow the Supplement Facts on the tub.
- No stimulants, hormones or botanicals. It is a sports amino-acid powder, not a 'sexual enhancement' product, which is the category where FDA's hidden-drug notices cluster [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications].

**Interactions**

- Nitrates (nitroglycerin, isosorbide mono- or dinitrate) and other NO donors, including amyl nitrite 'poppers': do not combine, because blood pressure may fall too low. Arginine or citrulline may potentiate nitrates and vasodilators [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine-with-sildenafil-232-0-2061-0.html?professional=1].
- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil, vardenafil): additive blood-pressure lowering. Drugs.com rates arginine + sildenafil as moderate and advises monitoring blood pressure and heart rate. Trials that deliberately combined 2.5-3 g arginine with tadalafil or sildenafil reported more mild GI effects but no serious events [https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine-with-sildenafil-232-0-2061-0.html?professional=1; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31267684].
- Antihypertensives (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, beta-blockers, calcium-channel blockers, diuretics) and alpha-blockers (doxazosin, prazosin, tamsulosin; Drugs.com rates tamsulosin as moderate): additive hypotension [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine; https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/l-arginine-with-tamsulosin-232-4042-2146-0.html?professional=1].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (warfarin, DOACs, clopidogrel, aspirin): Mayo Clinic lists a possible rise in bleeding risk with arginine [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Diabetes drugs: arginine may lower blood sugar, so doses may need adjusting; its effect with insulin is unpredictable [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://www.drugs.com/npp/l-arginine.html].
- Potassium-sparing diuretics (spironolactone, amiloride, triamterene): risk of high blood potassium [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Isoproterenol: blood pressure may fall too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Thyroid drugs, SSRIs, MAOIs and sedatives: Mayo Clinic and Cleveland Clinic list no interaction with arginine [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone with a recent heart attack [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with coronary artery disease or heart failure, unless a cardiologist agrees. NOW's own arginine tablet page on Nahdi warns against use with coronary artery disease or after a heart attack [https://www.nahdionline.com/ar-sa/now-foods-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets/pdp/101506676; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Men taking nitrates or potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with low blood pressure, or on several blood-pressure drugs, an alpha-blocker or a PDE5 inhibitor, unless a clinician supervises.
- People with asthma or significant allergies [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]. People with recurrent herpes simplex or glaucoma should ask a doctor first, as NOW's Canadian L-arginine label advises [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets].
- People with guanidinoacetate methyltransferase deficiency [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Men with new, unexplained ED who have not had a cardiovascular check, because ED often comes 2-3 years before coronary disease [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16854949/].

**Stop signals**

- Dizziness, light-headedness or fainting, which suggest blood pressure is too low.
- Chest pain, breathlessness or new palpitations: stop and get emergency care.
- Hives, facial or throat swelling, or wheezing: stop and get urgent care [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Persistent diarrhoea, nausea, bloating or heartburn; a gout flare; worsening asthma [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- A cold-sore (herpes) outbreak, given the herpes caution on NOW's arginine label [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets].
- Being prescribed nitrates, a potassium-sparing diuretic or a new blood-pressure drug: stop and review with the prescriber.
- No noticeable improvement after about 3 months, the length of the longest arginine-alone trials [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3].

Those summaries are not reliable on their own: the same kind of summary called Kaged Citrulline 'no longer certified', while the directly captured Kaged page shows it certified (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/kaged/citrulline).

The conflicting myGP panel (10 g serving = 5 g + 5 g, 34 servings) is internally consistent (34 x 10 g = 340 g), so it may be a real label variant rather than a typo (https://store.mygp.com/products/arginine-and-citrulline-powder-now-foods-340-grams). The 1:1 ratio is the same in both versions.
  - No reformulation evidence found.

- *safety-evidence lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*
  - certification_verified is set to true, but the batch details came only from a search-index copy. The entry itself notes that PODIUM's indexed copy lagged behind its withdrawal, so the status should read 'indirect' until the tub's batch ID is matched on the live page.
  - Hidden-drug screening is overstated: Informed Sport screens for sport-banned substances, while PDE5 inhibitors are not WADA-prohibited and BSCG lists them as non-sport drugs it adds. Read the result as 'sport-banned drugs only; PDE5 unconfirmed'.
  - Pairing this powder with separate Pycnogenol is an untested do-it-yourself regimen. Prelox used arginine aspartate with no citrulline, and Prelox R added roburins. Cormio was fixed-order and single-blind.
  - Missed interaction (inference): ACE inhibitors and ARBs also raise potassium, so check potassium when adding 1.5-3 g of arginine a day, especially with reduced kidney function.
