### Report rank 4: NOW Foods (NOW Sports) — NOW Sports L-Arginine Powder, unflavored (1 lb / 454 g; also 1 kg)

- **Verifier verdict:** recommend — A single fully disclosed ingredient listed by Informed Sport (batches tested through June 2026) that lets a man take exactly the 3 g or 6 g arginine doses of the positive ED trials, provided he checks which label version he has and splits the dose.
- **Product as verified:** NOW Foods (NOW Sports) — NOW Sports L-Arginine Powder, unflavored (1 lb / 454 g; also 1 kg)
- **Product page:** https://www.vitacost.com/products/now-foods-sports-l-arginine-powder-1-lb-454-g-369
- **Where sold:** US (Vitacost); Canada (Well.ca, Vitasave); New Zealand (NetPharmacy); Australia (Catch); Saudi Arabia (Nahdi, 1 kg).
- **Label verified:** yes. US Supplement Facts as reproduced by Vitacost and Nahdi (consistent: 2 level tsp about 6 g = 6 g L-arginine, about 76 servings per 454 g). Canadian listings (Vitasave, Well.ca) show 4.6 g per 2 tsp (about 2.3 g per teaspoon). NOW's own page and DSLD could not be opened this session.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving size 2 level teaspoons (about 6 g): L-arginine (free-form) 6 g; 30 kcal; about 76 servings per 454 g; unflavored. Canadian label version: 4.6 g per 2 teaspoons, so check which label you have. The label's suggested-use wording was not captured. For a Prelox replacement: 1 level teaspoon (about 3 g) daily, or up to 2 teaspoons (about 6 g) split into 2-3 doses after meals as in the 6 g trial.
- **Dose versus trials (matches):** 1 level tsp (about 3 g) = El-Wakeel 2020's 3 g/day [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31267684] and just above Gallo 2020's 2.5 g/day (IIEF-EF +3.1, +5.4 in mild ED) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/]. 2 level tsp (about 6 g, the label serving) = Menafra 2022's 6 g/day (2 g three times daily after meals for 3 months, 98 men with vasculogenic ED; IIEF-6 median 20 to 24; adverse events 5.9% vs 4.3% on placebo, none clinically relevant) [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3]; Chen 1999 used 5 g/day [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10233492/]. Single doses of 3-6 g rarely cause GI effects [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17513449/]. Against Prelox: even 1 tsp is roughly double the ~1.6-1.7 g arginine in Prelox's trial dose (my calculation from 2.8-3 g arginine aspartate [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/]); there is no Pycnogenol.
- **Certification:** Informed Sport. Listing 'NOW Sports L-Arginine Powder': date certified 28-Feb-2018; about 20 batches; latest 3461663 tested 03-Jun-2026 (expires 31-May-2030). Seen through the search-engine index of the certifier's own database page on 8 Nov 2026; the indexed copy still showed batch 3267310 (expired 30-Sep-2026), so it predates October 2026, and the live page could not be reopened this session. Informed Sport tests every batch before release for more than 285 substances banned in sport using ISO 17025 methods [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice]; it is a drug-screening programme, but whether its panel includes PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil-type drugs) was not confirmed, whereas BSCG's published menu names tadalafil, vardenafil and many sildenafil analogues [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725]. Certification is lot-specific: match the batch number on the container to the database. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/now-foods/now-sports-l-arginine-powder; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** NOW Foods / NOW Health Group (US manufacturer). Found: a 2005 FDA warning letter over possible quintozene (pesticide) contamination of one lot of American ginseng [https://www.supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/now-responds-to-fda-warning-letter]; a 2016 voluntary recall of six unrelated supplements (about 165 units) after a label-printer error [https://www.Drugs.com/fda/now-health-group-dietary-supplements-recall-mislabeling-13846.html]; an earlier voluntary recall of a phosphatidylserine product that did not meet its label claim [https://wholefoodsmagazine.com/keywords/12602-recall]; and, per a recall aggregator, 2025 food recalls of NOW Nutritional Yeast Powder (Class III, May 2025, terminated) and organic raw cashews (Class II, Sept 2025) [https://recallcheck.net/brand/now-foods; https://recallcheck.net/food-recalls/5197]. None found for any NOW arginine or citrulline product, and the searches surfaced no FDA hidden-drug (tainted product) notice for NOW, although FDA's tainted-products database itself was not queried. Searched: web search 'NOW Foods recall OR warning letter FDA 2024 2025 2026 supplement' (8 Nov 2026). Not searched: FDA's warning-letter and enforcement-report databases directly, Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. Live re-checks were impossible in this pass (WebSearch budget exhausted; fda.gov, sport.wetestyoutrust.com, nowfoods.com, kaged.com, DSLD and Europe PMC blocked; Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC), so these findings rest on the workflow's 8 Nov 2026 discovery reads and the project's earlier notes.
- **Evidence summary:** Same arginine evidence as the NOW tablets: modest benefit at 2.5-6 g/day in mild-to-moderate ED [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/30770070; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/; https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3], the only single nutraceutical with significant efficacy in a 2024 network meta-analysis [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/39279185], EAU weak recommendation [https://uroweb.org/guidelines/sexual-and-reproductive-health/chapter/management-of-erectile-dysfunction]. The powder is the practical way to follow the Menafra 6 g/day regimen, the only placebo-controlled arginine-alone RCT at that dose with validated ED scores.

**Safety risks**

- Post-heart-attack mortality signal: in VINTAGE MI (L-arginine 3 g three times daily = 9 g/day for 6 months after a first STEMI) 6 arginine patients (8.6%) died vs none on placebo and enrolment was closed; authors: 'L-arginine should not be recommended following acute myocardial infarction' [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/]. Mayo and Cleveland Clinic both say not to use after a recent heart attack [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Lowers blood pressure; with nitrates the drop can be dangerous, and it adds to blood-pressure drugs and PDE5 inhibitors [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Gastrointestinal effects (nausea, abdominal pain, diarrhea, bloating) [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; single doses of 3-6 g rarely provoke them, most occur at single doses above 9 g and divided doses help [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17513449/].
- Other reported effects: gout, headache, allergic reactions, airway inflammation or worse asthma [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; insomnia in 5% at 2.5 g/day [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/].
- Long-term safety: NIH ODS says up to 9 g/day is safe and well tolerated for several days or weeks, with GI upset and slightly lower blood pressure at 9-30 g/day [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/]; studies have lasted up to about 3 months [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-Consumer/].
- High potassium if combined with potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- The label serving (6 g) is double the 3 g that already reaches the positive-trial range; higher daily amounts raise GI-upset and blood-pressure effects, and VINTAGE MI used 9 g/day [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/].
- Label versions differ (6 g vs 4.6 g per 2 tsp); teaspoon dosing is approximate.
- No stimulants, hormones or botanicals; single amino acid not marketed for sexual enhancement.

**Interactions**

- Nitrates (nitroglycerin, isosorbide mononitrate/dinitrate): do not combine; blood pressure may fall too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine]; Drugs.com: arginine may potentiate nitrates and other NO donors [https://www.drugs.com/npp/l-arginine.html].
- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil): Mayo warns sildenafil plus L-arginine may drop blood pressure too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; Drugs.com rates arginine + sildenafil as a moderate interaction and advises monitoring blood pressure and heart rate [https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine-with-sildenafil-232-0-2061-0.html?professional=1]; deliberate combination trials found more dyspepsia/gastritis but no significant rise in adverse events overall [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31267684; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/33587304].
- Antihypertensives (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, beta-blockers, calcium-channel blockers, alpha-blockers such as doxazosin and prazosin; tamsulosin rated moderate by Drugs.com): additive blood-pressure lowering [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/l-arginine-with-tamsulosin-232-4042-2146-0.html?professional=1].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (e.g., warfarin, aspirin): may increase bleeding risk [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Diabetes drugs: L-arginine may lower blood sugar so doses may need adjusting [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; effect with insulin is unpredictable [https://www.drugs.com/npp/l-arginine.html].
- Potassium-sparing diuretics (amiloride, spironolactone, triamterene): do not combine, risk of hyperkalemia [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Isoproterenol: blood pressure may fall too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction is listed by Mayo Clinic or Cleveland Clinic [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone with a recent heart attack (VINTAGE MI; Mayo; Cleveland Clinic) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with coronary artery disease or heart failure unless a cardiologist approves (NOW's Arabic L-arginine tablet page on Nahdi says not to use with coronary artery disease or after a heart attack; Cleveland Clinic lists heart failure as a serious reaction) [https://www.nahdionline.com/ar-sa/now-foods-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets/pdp/101506676; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Men who take nitrates, or potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with low blood pressure, or on several blood-pressure drugs or a PDE5 inhibitor, unless a clinician supervises [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- People with asthma or significant allergies (may worsen) [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- People with recurrent herpes simplex or glaucoma should ask a physician first (caution printed on NOW's Canadian L-arginine tablet label) [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets].
- Guanidinoacetate methyltransferase deficiency; adults over 65 without provider approval [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Men with new, unexplained ED who have not had a cardiovascular check, because ED predicts cardiovascular events [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16414947/].

**Stop signals**

- Dizziness, light-headedness or fainting (blood pressure too low).
- Chest pain, breathlessness or new palpitations: stop and seek emergency care.
- Hives, facial or throat swelling, or wheezing: stop and seek urgent care [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Persistent diarrhea, nausea, bloating or heartburn; a gout flare; worsening asthma [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- A cold-sore (herpes) outbreak, given the herpes caution on NOW's Canadian arginine label [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets].
- Being prescribed nitrates, a potassium-sparing diuretic or a new blood-pressure drug: stop and review with the prescriber.
- No noticeable benefit after about 3 months (the length of the longest arginine-alone trials) [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3].

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - Never opened directly. All listing data (certified 28-Feb-2018, about 20 batches, latest 3461663 tested 03-Jun-2026) come from one search-engine summary of https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/now-foods/now-sports-l-arginine-powder that still showed batch 3267310, which expired on 30-Sep-2026; the snapshot therefore predates October 2026 and today's status is unconfirmed.
  - So 'certification_verified: true' overstates the evidence. It is an indexed, pre-October listing (NOW itself is still active in the programme: its tablet listing shows an October 2026 test).
  - Label versions conflict: US copies give 6 g per 2 level teaspoons, Canadian copies 4.6 g per 2 teaspoons (about 2.3 g per teaspoon), so '1 teaspoon = about 3 g' can overstate the Canadian tub by roughly 30% (https://www.vitasave.ca/l-arginine-powder.html).
  - Directions were never captured. No other reformulation evidence.

- *safety-evidence lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*
  - Certification only checked indirectly: batch 3461663 (tested 03-Jun-2026) came from a search-index copy that still showed a batch that expired on 30-Sep-2026, so the copy is stale. Treat it as probable but unconfirmed until the tub's batch is matched on the live page.
  - 'Matches' for the dose depends on the label. US labels give 6 g per 2 level teaspoons but Canadian labels give 4.6 g, so dosing by spoon can be off by about 25%. Weigh doses, and split 6 g into 2-3 doses after meals, as in Menafra; single doses above about 6 g make stomach upset more likely.
  - PDE5 caveat on the Informed Sport screen: PDE5 inhibitors are not WADA-prohibited, and whether they are tested is unconfirmed.
  - Rhim 2019 pooled arginine-only trials with combination products. Menafra's 6 g/day trial is the relevant placebo-controlled anchor.
  - Missed interaction (inference): ACE inhibitors and ARBs also raise potassium. Check potassium when combining them with 6 g/day of arginine, especially with reduced kidney function.

### Report rank 5: Holland & Barrett (Precision Engineered) — Precision Engineered L-Arginine 1000mg tablets (100)

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — An Informed Sport-listed single-ingredient arginine (batches tested through July 2026) that is the easiest certified option for a UK or EU buyer, but its directions and salt form were not captured and Holland & Barrett's regulatory record was not checked, so it cannot be rated above acceptable.
- **Product as verified:** Holland & Barrett (Precision Engineered) — Precision Engineered L-Arginine 1000mg tablets (100)
- **Product page:** https://www.hollandandbarrett.com/shop/product/larginine-1000mg-100-tablets-6100004716
- **Where sold:** UK and Ireland (Holland & Barrett online delivers only to the UK and Republic of Ireland); EU: the 1000 mg SKU is listed on Holland & Barrett's Spain and Cyprus sites (the Greek site lists the 500 mg capsules). Gulf: franchise stores in the UAE (Al Khayyat Investments partnership renewed May 2025), Kuwait and Qatar (Talabat listings) and Saudi Arabia (Jeddah store opened 2018); whether they stock this SKU was not verified.
- **Label verified:** no. Holland & Barrett's own UK, Cyprus and Spain product pages (read through the search index): 1,000 mg L-arginine per tablet on the Cyprus nutrition panel; the UK overview says 'per capsule', apparently a copy error. Directions, serving size, other ingredients and whether the 1,000 mg is arginine base or the HCl salt were not captured, so the panel is only partly verified.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** 1 tablet = L-arginine 1,000 mg; 100 tablets; vegan/vegetarian. Daily directions and serving size were not captured. For a Prelox replacement: 2 tablets (2 g) match Prelox's arginine content and 3 tablets (3 g) reach the positive-trial range, if the pack directions and a clinician allow.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** 3 tablets = 3 g/day (El-Wakeel 2020) [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31267684], above Gallo 2020's 2.5 g/day [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/]; 5-6 tablets = the 5-6 g/day of Chen 1999 and Menafra 2022 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10233492/; https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3]. If the pack directs 1 tablet a day, 1 g/day is below the 1.5 g/day that failed in Klotz 1999 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10743698/]. If the 1,000 mg is arginine HCl rather than base, each tablet gives about 830 mg arginine (my calculation). Against Prelox: no Pycnogenol; 2 tablets roughly equal Prelox's ~1.6-1.7 g arginine (calculated from 2.8-3 g arginine aspartate [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/]).
- **Certification:** Informed Sport. Listing 'L-Arginine 1000mg' (Precision Engineered by Holland & Barrett): date certified 20-Nov-2024; 15 batches; latest 00810401 tested 16-Jul-2026; earliest 77044202 and 77044302 tested 19-Nov-2024; batch expiries from 28-Feb-2027 to 31-May-2028. Seen through the search-engine index of the certifier's own database page on 8 Nov 2026 (a second, general search saw the page without these details); the live page could not be reopened this session. Holland & Barrett's October-2024 announcement with Informed Sport says every batch of its certified products is tested for more than 285 banned substances [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/news/holland-barrett-marks-one-largest-informed-sport-certifications-date]. Informed Sport tests every batch before release for more than 285 substances banned in sport using ISO 17025 methods [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice]; it is a drug-screening programme, but whether its panel includes PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil-type drugs) was not confirmed, whereas BSCG's published menu names tadalafil, vardenafil and many sildenafil analogues [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725]. Certification is lot-specific: match the batch number on the container to the database. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/precision-engineered-holland-barrett/l-arginine-1000mg; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Holland & Barrett (UK health retailer) own-brand sports range; the contract manufacturer is not named. No adverse record was gathered, but none was looked for: FDA, UK FSA/MHRA, Health Canada and TGA alerts and recalls for Holland & Barrett or this product were not searched in this verification pass, so 'none found' applies only to the Informed Sport listing and certification announcement reviewed. Live re-checks were impossible in this pass (WebSearch budget exhausted; fda.gov, sport.wetestyoutrust.com, nowfoods.com, kaged.com, DSLD and Europe PMC blocked; Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC), so these findings rest on the workflow's 8 Nov 2026 discovery reads and the project's earlier notes.
- **Evidence summary:** Same arginine evidence as the NOW tablets: modest benefit at 2.5-6 g/day [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/30770070; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/; https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3], significant in a 2024 network meta-analysis [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/39279185], EAU weak recommendation for mild ED [https://uroweb.org/guidelines/sexual-and-reproductive-health/chapter/management-of-erectile-dysfunction]; no Pycnogenol, so it replaces only the arginine half of Prelox.

**Safety risks**

- Post-heart-attack mortality signal: in VINTAGE MI (L-arginine 3 g three times daily = 9 g/day for 6 months after a first STEMI) 6 arginine patients (8.6%) died vs none on placebo and enrolment was closed; authors: 'L-arginine should not be recommended following acute myocardial infarction' [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/]. Mayo and Cleveland Clinic both say not to use after a recent heart attack [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Lowers blood pressure; with nitrates the drop can be dangerous, and it adds to blood-pressure drugs and PDE5 inhibitors [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Gastrointestinal effects (nausea, abdominal pain, diarrhea, bloating) [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; single doses of 3-6 g rarely provoke them, most occur at single doses above 9 g and divided doses help [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17513449/].
- Other reported effects: gout, headache, allergic reactions, airway inflammation or worse asthma [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; insomnia in 5% at 2.5 g/day [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/].
- Long-term safety: NIH ODS says up to 9 g/day is safe and well tolerated for several days or weeks, with GI upset and slightly lower blood pressure at 9-30 g/day [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/]; studies have lasted up to about 3 months [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-Consumer/].
- High potassium if combined with potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Pack directions and salt form were not verified, so the delivered arginine per day is uncertain until the box is read.
- No stimulants, hormones or botanicals; single amino acid not marketed for sexual enhancement.

**Interactions**

- Nitrates (nitroglycerin, isosorbide mononitrate/dinitrate): do not combine; blood pressure may fall too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine]; Drugs.com: arginine may potentiate nitrates and other NO donors [https://www.drugs.com/npp/l-arginine.html].
- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil): Mayo warns sildenafil plus L-arginine may drop blood pressure too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; Drugs.com rates arginine + sildenafil as a moderate interaction and advises monitoring blood pressure and heart rate [https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine-with-sildenafil-232-0-2061-0.html?professional=1]; deliberate combination trials found more dyspepsia/gastritis but no significant rise in adverse events overall [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/31267684; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/33587304].
- Antihypertensives (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, beta-blockers, calcium-channel blockers, alpha-blockers such as doxazosin and prazosin; tamsulosin rated moderate by Drugs.com): additive blood-pressure lowering [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/l-arginine-with-tamsulosin-232-4042-2146-0.html?professional=1].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (e.g., warfarin, aspirin): may increase bleeding risk [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Diabetes drugs: L-arginine may lower blood sugar so doses may need adjusting [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]; effect with insulin is unpredictable [https://www.drugs.com/npp/l-arginine.html].
- Potassium-sparing diuretics (amiloride, spironolactone, triamterene): do not combine, risk of hyperkalemia [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Isoproterenol: blood pressure may fall too low [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction is listed by Mayo Clinic or Cleveland Clinic [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone with a recent heart attack (VINTAGE MI; Mayo; Cleveland Clinic) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with coronary artery disease or heart failure unless a cardiologist approves (NOW's Arabic L-arginine tablet page on Nahdi says not to use with coronary artery disease or after a heart attack; Cleveland Clinic lists heart failure as a serious reaction) [https://www.nahdionline.com/ar-sa/now-foods-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets/pdp/101506676; https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Men who take nitrates, or potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with low blood pressure, or on several blood-pressure drugs or a PDE5 inhibitor, unless a clinician supervises [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- People with asthma or significant allergies (may worsen) [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- People with recurrent herpes simplex or glaucoma should ask a physician first (caution printed on NOW's Canadian L-arginine tablet label) [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets].
- Guanidinoacetate methyltransferase deficiency; adults over 65 without provider approval [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Men with new, unexplained ED who have not had a cardiovascular check, because ED predicts cardiovascular events [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16414947/].

**Stop signals**

- Dizziness, light-headedness or fainting (blood pressure too low).
- Chest pain, breathlessness or new palpitations: stop and seek emergency care.
- Hives, facial or throat swelling, or wheezing: stop and seek urgent care [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine].
- Persistent diarrhea, nausea, bloating or heartburn; a gout flare; worsening asthma [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- A cold-sore (herpes) outbreak, given the herpes caution on NOW's Canadian arginine label [https://vitasave.ca/products/now-l-arginine-1000-mg-120-tablets].
- Being prescribed nitrates, a potassium-sparing diuretic or a new blood-pressure drug: stop and review with the prescriber.
- No noticeable benefit after about 3 months (the length of the longest arginine-alone trials) [https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40618-021-01704-3].

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: acceptable*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - Listing data (certified 20-Nov-2024; 15 batches, latest 00810401 tested 16-Jul-2026, earliest 77044202 and 77044302 tested 19-Nov-2024) come only from a search-engine summary of https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/precision-engineered-holland-barrett/l-arginine-1000mg. It was never opened directly and no expired batch is shown, so the snapshot date is unknown.
  - Another search summary claimed H&B's Informed Sport certification is explicit only for its 500 mg capsules. The indexed 1000 mg listing with its own batches contradicts that, but the listing's formulation field was not captured.
  - The label is still incomplete: directions, salt form (base or HCl) and excipients are uncaptured, and H&B's UK copy says 'per capsule' for a tablet product.

  - Holland & Barrett's own regulatory record (FSA and MHRA recalls) was never searched, so 'no adverse record' means only that no search was done, as the verifier admitted.
  - The PDE5 caveat on the Informed Sport screen, the Rhim pooling caveat and the ACE inhibitor/ARB potassium caution (inference) apply as for the other arginine products.

### Report rank 6: Healthy Origins — Healthy Origins Performance L-Citrulline powder (fermented, KQ; 10.6 oz / 300 g)

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — A clean single-ingredient fermented citrulline at a trial-matched dose with a ConsumerLab approval, but ConsumerLab checks identity and contaminants rather than hidden drugs, the brand's regulatory record was not checked, and it is hard to find in the Gulf, so Kaged's Informed Sport citrulline is the stronger pick.
- **Product as verified:** Healthy Origins — Healthy Origins Performance L-Citrulline powder (fermented, KQ; 10.6 oz / 300 g)
- **Product page:** https://www.vitacost.com/products/healthy-origins-performance-l-citrulline-10-6-oz-300-g-157674
- **Where sold:** US (Vitacost, Swanson); EU (Smeets & Graas, Netherlands; desertcart Cyprus). No UAE or Saudi listing was found (iHerb, Nahdi and Life Pharmacy searched).
- **Label verified:** yes. Supplement Facts as reproduced by Vitacost and Swanson (US) and Smeets & Graas (EU label), consistent: 1 level scoop (3 g) = 3,000 mg L-citrulline (fermented, KQ), 100 servings. A desertcart listing titles the product '2,000 mg per serving', possibly an older label; the earlier product name was 'L-Citrulline (American-Made)'.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving size 1 level scoop (3 g): L-citrulline (fermented, 'KQ') 3 g (3,000 mg); 100 servings per 300 g; single ingredient. The label's suggested-use wording was not captured. For a Prelox replacement: 1/2 scoop (1.5 g, the ED-trial dose) to 1 scoop (3 g) daily.
- **Dose versus trials (matches):** 1/2 scoop = 1.5 g, the Cormio 2011 ED dose (1.5 g/day for 1 month, single-blind, 24 men, 50% vs 8.3% improved erection hardness) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/]; 1 scoop = 3 g, double that dose but within the 3-8 g/day of the blood-pressure studies and the 3-6 g/day 'safe dosage' cited by Cleveland Clinic [https://health.clevelandclinic.org/citrulline-benefits], and below Schwedhelm 2008's top regimen of 3 g twice daily [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17662090/]. Against Prelox: no Pycnogenol; Prelox R used 1.2 g citrulline with 1.92 g arginine and 80 mg Pycnogenol [https://www.oatext.com/review-influence-of-combinations-of-pycnogenolr-and-l-arginine-aspartate-on-mens-virility-and-fertility.php].
- **Certification:** ConsumerLab Approved (identity, strength and purity; not a drug-screening programme). ConsumerLab's own Healthy Origins brand page states 'Healthy Origins L-Citrulline is Approved in our L-Citrulline Supplements Review' and that it was tested through ConsumerLab's Voluntary Quality Certification Program; approval requires 100-120% of the labeled L-citrulline by HPLC, heavy-metal limits and FDA labeling compliance [https://www.consumerlab.com/methods/l-citrulline-supplements/l-citrulline/]; the review page was last updated 25 Jun 2026, and one product in the review was not accurately labeled (not identified in the free text). The size/form and test date are behind ConsumerLab's paywall; this 300 g powder is the only Healthy Origins L-citrulline found. Seen through the search index of consumerlab.com; the live page could not be reopened this session. ConsumerLab does not screen for hidden drugs such as PDE5 inhibitors. (certifier page: https://www.consumerlab.com/healthy-origins/?type=review; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Healthy Origins (US supplement brand); the citrulline is labeled as fermented 'KQ' L-citrulline. No adverse record was gathered, but none was looked for: FDA warning letters, recalls, hidden-drug notices and Health Canada, TGA and MHRA alerts for Healthy Origins were not searched in this verification pass; the ConsumerLab brand page reviewed showed three Healthy Origins products tested through its certification programme and no warnings. Live re-checks were impossible in this pass (WebSearch budget exhausted; fda.gov, sport.wetestyoutrust.com, nowfoods.com, kaged.com, DSLD and Europe PMC blocked; Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC), so these findings rest on the workflow's 8 Nov 2026 discovery reads and the project's earlier notes.
- **Evidence summary:** Same citrulline evidence as Kaged: one small single-blind pilot for ED [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/], not identified as effective in the 2024 network meta-analysis [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/39279185], good pharmacological rationale [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17662090/]; ConsumerLab itself says clinical studies of citrulline for exercise and cardiovascular uses show only modest to no benefit [https://www.consumerlab.com/reviews/l-citrulline-supplements/l-citrulline/].

**Safety risks**

- Lowers blood pressure; adds to blood-pressure drugs and some ED medicines, and is risky with nitrates [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794; https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- Gastrointestinal upset: bloating, cramping, diarrhea, sweating [https://health.clevelandclinic.org/citrulline-benefits]; stomach discomfort in 6 of 41 weight lifters [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/]; ConsumerLab notes occasional GI upset and heartburn [https://www.consumerlab.com/reviews/l-citrulline-supplements/l-citrulline/].
- Long-term safety not adequately assessed for months-long use; short-term studies up to 6 g/day for 4 weeks found no adverse effects [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/ExerciseAndAthleticPerformance-HealthProfessional/]; single doses of 2-15 g caused no side effects in 8 healthy men [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17953788/].
- Serious allergic reactions are possible but uncommon [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- ConsumerLab approval does not test for hidden drugs, though a single amino acid not marketed for sexual enhancement carries a low a priori adulteration risk.
- Label versions may differ (3 g vs '2,000 mg per serving' listing); read the tub.

**Interactions**

- Nitrates (nitroglycerin, isosorbide): avoid; additive blood-pressure fall [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline]; Drugs.com: L-arginine or L-citrulline may potentiate nitrates and vasodilators [https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine-with-sildenafil-232-0-2061-0.html?professional=1].
- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil, vardenafil, avanafil): listed as interacting (additive blood-pressure lowering) [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline; https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794]; a small crossover adding 800 mg citrulline + 300 mg resveratrol to PDE5i users reported no adverse events [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6302103/].
- Antihypertensives: blood pressure may go too low [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794; https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- Surgery: stop at least 2 weeks before scheduled surgery [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].
- Anticoagulants/antiplatelets, diabetes drugs, thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction listed in the WebMD or Natural Medicines summaries reviewed [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline; https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men who take nitrates [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- Men with low blood pressure, or on several blood-pressure drugs or a PDE5 inhibitor, unless a clinician supervises [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].
- Men with a past heart attack, heart or blood-vessel disease, or kidney or liver problems should clear it with a clinician first [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline]; because citrulline raises plasma arginine more than arginine itself [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17662090/], the post-heart-attack arginine caution [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/] is prudent here too (inference).
- Anyone with surgery scheduled within 2 weeks [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].
- Men with new, unexplained ED before a cardiovascular check [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16414947/].

**Stop signals**

- Dizziness, light-headedness or fainting (blood pressure too low).
- Chest pain, breathlessness or new palpitations: stop and seek emergency care.
- Hives, swelling of face/lips/throat, wheezing or racing heart: stop and get help [https://www.webmd.com/vitamins-supplements/l-citrulline].
- Persistent bloating, cramping, diarrhea or heartburn [https://health.clevelandclinic.org/citrulline-benefits].
- Being prescribed nitrates or a new blood-pressure drug, or surgery being scheduled: stop and review [https://www.vital.ly/trc/L-citrulline/monograph=794].
- No noticeable benefit after 1-3 months (the ED study lasted 1 month) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21195829/].

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — corrected; downgrade to: acceptable*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - ConsumerLab approval is real but was seen only through search summaries of ConsumerLab's brand page ('Healthy Origins L-Citrulline is Approved'). It was tested through ConsumerLab's paid Voluntary Quality Certification Program, and the tested size, lot and date are behind the paywall (https://www.consumerlab.com/healthy-origins/?type=review).
  - One index snapshot titles the review 'L-Citrulline Review 2023' while the page shows a 25-Jun-2026 update, so the test most likely dates from the 2023 cycle. The approval covers that sample, not current lots.
  - The product has been renamed ('L-Citrulline (American-Made)' to 'Performance L-Citrulline') and one listing still says '2,000 mg per serving', so the approved item may have carried an older label than the 3 g-scoop version described.
  - ConsumerLab checks identity, amount and heavy metals; it does not screen for PDE5 inhibitors or other drugs.

- *safety-evidence lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - The ConsumerLab approval was read through a search-index copy. The size, form and test date are behind the paywall, so the approval may predate the current 300 g label. ConsumerLab checks identity and contaminants, not drugs.
  - Cormio 2011, the only citrulline ED study, ran in a fixed order and was single-blind, so the dose match carries little evidential weight.
  - A genuine positive: identity testing matters for citrulline. Examine reports a 2014 recall of a citrulline product that contained no citrulline, and citrulline-malate products that were below their label claim.
  - Healthy Origins' record with the FDA and Health Canada was never searched.

### Report rank 7: Pharma Nord (Horphag Research licensee) — Pharma Nord Prelox tablets (60 or 180)

Two or more verification batches checked this product independently; all 3 records are kept so their differences stay visible.

#### Verification record 1 of 3

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — This is the closest like-for-like replacement for LE Prelox: the same licensed actives with every amount printed, and 4 tablets a day reproduces the RCT dose exactly; it stays at 'acceptable' only because no certifier lists it and Pharma Nord's regulatory record could not be re-checked in this run.
- **Product as verified:** Pharma Nord (Horphag Research licensee) — Pharma Nord Prelox tablets (60 or 180)
- **Product page:** https://www.pharmanord.co.uk/all-products/mens-health/prelox
- **Where sold:** UK: Pharma Nord UK direct (Holland & Barrett listing reported by discovery). EU: Horphag's licensee map names Pharma Nord as the Prelox licensee for the UK, Germany, Austria, Belgium, Finland, Lithuania, Netherlands, Portugal and Sweden (https://www.prelox.com/shop, prior project research); discovery also found it in German and Austrian pharmacies, France/Belgium (Redcare) and Estonia. Gulf: not available; Pharma Nord's country selector lists no GCC country (prior research) and discovery found no Gulf retailer (Life Pharmacy, Nahdi, Aster, Boots ME). The nearest Middle East licensee on Horphag's map is Legend Pharma (Jordan), a different label that was not checked. US: the Prelox licence there belongs to Life Extension and others, so this version is not sold in the US.
- **Label verified:** yes. Pharma Nord UK product page (https://www.pharmanord.co.uk/all-products/mens-health/prelox): verbatim Supplement Facts panel, ingredient list and directions captured on 7 Nov 2026 in prior project research (research_notes/Prelox vs Performer 8 ExtenZe Semenax/life_extension_prelox_and_performer_8.md). Discovery's reading of the same panel agrees. Not re-fetched in this run, because this session's network policy blocks pharmanord.co.uk.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving size 2 tablets. Panel (verbatim): 'Two Tablets Contain: Prelox Proprietary Blend 1540mg | L-Arginine aspartate 1400mg | Taurine 100mg | Pycnogenol 40mg'. Although the panel says 'blend', every component is itemised (1,400 + 100 + 40 = 1,540 mg), so nothing is hidden. Per tablet: 700 mg L-arginine aspartate, 50 mg taurine, 20 mg Pycnogenol. Directions (verbatim): '2 tablets twice per day for the first 2 weeks and 1 tablet twice per day thereafter ... To be taken with food.' Daily amounts, days 1-14 (4 tablets): L-arginine aspartate 2,800 mg (about 1.6 g free L-arginine; my calculation from molecular weights, 174.2/307.3), taurine 200 mg, Pycnogenol 80 mg. Thereafter (2 tablets): L-arginine aspartate 1,400 mg (about 0.8 g L-arginine), taurine 100 mg, Pycnogenol 40 mg. Other ingredients (verbatim list): microcrystalline cellulose, silicon dioxide, mono- and diglycerides of fatty acids, croscarmellose sodium, magnesium stearate, glazing agents (hydroxypropyl cellulose, hydroxypropyl methylcellulose, talc, calcium carbonate), concentrated spirulina colour. No stimulant, hormone or other herb. Pack sizes: 60 and 180 tablets.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** Trial regimen: Ledda 2010 (124 men, 6 months) and Stanislavov 2008 (50 men, 1-month crossover) gave 2 tablets twice daily of a tablet holding 20 mg Pycnogenol + 700 mg L-arginine aspartate, i.e. 80 mg Pycnogenol + 2.8-3 g L-arginine aspartate per day (Tian 2023, Table 1: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/; Ledda: https://europepmc.org/article/MED/20184576). Pharma Nord's tablet has the same two actives per tablet plus 50 mg taurine, which the trial descriptions do not mention. Label days 1-14 (4 tablets/day) = 80 mg + 2,800 mg: an exact match. Label maintenance (2 tablets/day) = 40 mg + 1,400 mg: half the trial dose, close to Aoki 2012's low-dose regimen (60 mg Pycnogenol + 690 mg arginine + 552 mg aspartic acid for 8 weeks: IIEF-5 total improved, but the erectile domain was not significantly better than placebo; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21618639/). To reproduce Ledda and Stanislavov, a user would stay on 4 tablets/day, the label's own starting dose; this exceeds the label's maintenance direction, so agree it with a doctor. Free L-arginine at 4 tablets/day is about 1.6 g. That matches Stanislavov 2003's statement that 3 g arginine aspartate equals 1.7 g L-arginine (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12851125/). It is below the 2.5-6 g/day used in arginine-only trials, so the reported effect rests on the combination. Taurine (100-200 mg/day) has no ED trial basis.
- **Certification:** None. Discovery found no product-level certification in Informed Sport/Informed Choice, BSCG, NSF or USP. Pharma Nord's own statements ('Manufactured to pharmaceutical standards'; an in-house GMP plant in Vojens, Denmark) are company claims, not certifier listings. In this run no certifier database could be opened. (certifier page: none; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Maker: Pharma Nord ApS, Vojens, Denmark. The company says it manufactures in its own plant under GMP 'under the auspices of the Danish Medicines Agency' (company statement, https://shop.pharmanord.eu/welcome/produced-in-denmark, per discovery). Horphag Research owns the Prelox and Pycnogenol trademarks and names Pharma Nord as its European Prelox licensee (https://www.prelox.com/shop). In Germany and Austria it is sold as a food for special medical purposes ('ergaenzende bilanzierte Diaet') for the dietary management of ED, to be used under medical supervision (discovery; https://www.onlineapo.at/en/Prelox-tablets/RA5982982). Pharma Nord UK shows a Trustpilot score of 4.9/5 from 8,872 reviews (prior research). Regulatory: prior project research (8 Nov 2026) found no FDA, FTC, recall or hidden-drug notification naming Prelox. That check used a site-restricted fda.gov search plus keyword checks of FDA's 441-entry tainted sexual-enhancement list (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications). Discovery reported no recall, warning letter or hidden-drug report for Pharma Nord. VERIFICATION LIMIT, this run: the shared WebSearch call limit (200 calls per turn) was already reached, and this session's network allows only package registries and MCP servers. As a result pharmanord.co.uk did not resolve, and the MHRA/FSA, RASFF, Danish Veterinary and Food Administration, Health Canada and TGA databases could not be queried. I did not route around these limits. To finish the check, allow those hosts in the session's network settings (or raise CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION) and re-run this batch. Status: no red flag known; check incomplete.
- **Evidence summary:** There are three small double-blind RCTs of this two-ingredient formula, all with Horphag-linked authors. Ledda 2010 (124 men aged 30-50, moderate ED, 6 months): the IIEF erectile domain rose from 15.2 to 25.2 at 3 months and 27.1 at 6 months, against 15.1 to 19.1 and 19.0 on placebo; total testosterone rose from 15.9 to 18.9 nmol/L (https://europepmc.org/article/MED/20184576). Stanislavov 2008 (50 men, crossover, 1 month): erectile function was restored to normal, intercourse frequency doubled, cholesterol and blood pressure fell, and no unwanted effects were seen (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17703218/). Aoki 2012 (24 men, lower dose, 8 weeks): the IIEF-5 total improved (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21618639/). The Tian 2023 meta-analysis (3 RCTs, 184 men) found IIEF-EF +8.9 points vs placebo (95% CI 4.14-13.66; 2 trials) and no adverse events, but rated the risk of bias unclear to high (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/). Horphag says the crossover benefit largely faded after stopping (https://www.prelox.com/research). No data exist beyond 6 months, in men over 50 or in severe ED. The EAU gives only a weak recommendation for arginine or ginseng in mild ED and does not endorse Prelox (https://uroweb.org/guidelines/sexual-and-reproductive-health/chapter/management-of-erectile-dysfunction).

**Safety risks**

- Vasodilation effects: headache, flushing, dizziness or palpitations (Drugs.com L-arginine monograph, https://www.drugs.com/npp/l-arginine.html). Pharma Nord's German leaflet warns of facial flushing or headache at the start (per discovery, https://www.rathaus-apotheken.de/pimg/docs/00089537.pdf).
- Recent heart attack: an RCT of 9 g/day arginine after STEMI was stopped for excess deaths (8.6% vs 0%; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/). Mayo Clinic says arginine is not recommended after a recent heart attack (https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681). Prelox supplies much less (about 1.6 g/day), but the caution stands.
- Bleeding: Pycnogenol inhibits platelet aggregation; 100 mg matched 500 mg aspirin against smoking-induced aggregation (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10498385/; MSKCC, https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/pine-bark-extract). Mayo lists bleeding risk for arginine taken with blood thinners. Examine notes no increased bleeding across 27 Pycnogenol studies (https://examine.com/supplements/pycnogenol/).
- High potassium if combined with potassium-sparing diuretics (Mayo Clinic).
- Mild GI upset, insomnia, headache and dyspepsia: 11 adverse events per 100 men over 12 weeks at 2.5 g/day arginine, with insomnia at 5% (Gallo 2020, https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/).
- Possible prolonged erection: an unverified Pharma Nord customer review reports a 5-hour erection that needed A&E (prior project research, https://www.pharmanord.co.uk/all-products/mens-health/prelox).
- Taurine at 100-200 mg/day is far below EFSA's no-observed-adverse-effect level of 1,000 mg/kg body weight/day (https://www.efsa.europa.eu/en/news/efsa-adopts-opinion-two-ingredients-commonly-used-some-energy-drinks).
- No drug-screening certification, so the absence of hidden PDE5 inhibitors is not independently tested, although no adulteration report exists for Prelox.
- No safety data beyond 6 months of continuous use.

**Interactions**

- Nitrates and NO donors (nitroglycerin, isosorbide mono- or dinitrate, nicorandil, amyl nitrite 'poppers'): additive hypotension, so do not combine. Sources: Mayo Clinic, and Drugs.com, which says arginine or citrulline 'may potentiate the hypotensive effects of nitrates and vasodilators' (https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine-with-sildenafil-232-0-2061-0.html?professional=1).
- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil, vardenafil): additive blood-pressure lowering. Drugs.com rates arginine + sildenafil 'moderate, monitor'. In a 300-man trial, arginine 2.5 g + tadalafil 5 mg daily caused more mild side effects but no serious events (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/).
- Antihypertensives (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, beta-blockers, calcium-channel blockers) and alpha-blockers such as tamsulosin or doxazosin: additive hypotension (Cleveland Clinic, https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine; Drugs.com rates tamsulosin 'moderate', https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/l-arginine-with-tamsulosin-232-4042-2146-0.html?professional=1).
- Potassium-sparing diuretics (spironolactone, amiloride, triamterene): risk of high blood potassium (Mayo Clinic).
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (warfarin, apixaban, rivaroxaban, clopidogrel, aspirin, NSAIDs): increased bleeding risk (Mayo for arginine; MSKCC for pine bark).
- Diabetes drugs (insulin, sulfonylureas, metformin): arginine and Pycnogenol may lower blood glucose, so doses may need adjusting (Mayo; Examine).
- Isoproterenol: hypotension (Mayo Clinic).
- Thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction documented for arginine, Pycnogenol or taurine in the sources reviewed (Mayo, Drugs.com, MSKCC, Examine).

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone taking nitrates or other NO donors, including recreational 'poppers'.
- Men within 6 months of a heart attack, stroke or life-threatening arrhythmia, or with unstable angina or heart failure (VINTAGE MI; the cardiovascular exclusions in the sildenafil label, https://www.drugs.com/pro/viagra.html).
- Men with low resting blood pressure or uncontrolled high blood pressure. The German leaflet says to consult a doctor first if you have severe hypertension, heart problems or a serious illness, or take many medicines (per discovery).
- Men on anticoagulants or antiplatelets, or with a bleeding disorder, unless a doctor agrees; stop before planned surgery and tell the surgeon.
- Men on potassium-sparing diuretics or with kidney disease or electrolyte imbalance (Drugs.com lists electrolyte imbalance as arginine's disease interaction, https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine,l-arginine.html).
- Guanidinoacetate methyltransferase deficiency (Cleveland Clinic). Use caution with asthma or allergies (Mayo).
- Pine allergy.
- Men with new, unexplained ED who have not had a cardiovascular check: the AUA calls ED a risk marker for cardiovascular disease (https://www.auanet.org/guidelines-and-quality/guidelines/erectile-dysfunction-(ed)-guideline).

**Stop signals**

- Chest pain, palpitations, fainting or marked dizziness: stop and seek medical care.
- An erection lasting more than 4 hours: get emergency care (the standard PDE5-inhibitor warning, https://www.drugs.com/pro/viagra.html).
- Unusual bruising, nosebleeds, or blood in urine or stool.
- Headache, flushing or stomach upset that persists beyond the first 2 weeks.
- Low-blood-sugar symptoms if taking diabetes medicines.
- Rash, swelling or other allergic reaction.
- No gain of at least 4 points on the IIEF erectile domain (2 points if ED is mild) after 8-12 weeks at the trial dose: stop, as it is not working (minimal clinically important difference, Rosen 2011, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21855209/).

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).

'Manufactured to pharmaceutical standards' and the own GMP plant are company claims.

  - Staying on the trial regimen (4 tablets a day) goes beyond the label's maintenance direction and its 'do not exceed' line, so it needs a doctor's agreement.
  - No reformulation evidence; the pharmanord.eu table omits taurine while the UK and German copies list it, so check the pack in hand.

- *safety-evidence lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*
  - Missed caution: monographs based on Natural Medicines (WebMD) list an interaction with immunosuppressants and advise avoiding Pycnogenol in autoimmune disease, because it may stimulate immune activity (theoretical). This matters for men on ciclosporin, tacrolimus, methotrexate or biologics, or with MS, lupus or rheumatoid arthritis. From my own knowledge; the link was not re-opened this pass.
  - The trial match is slightly overstated. The Ledda and Stanislavov tablets held 20 mg Pycnogenol plus 700 mg L-arginine aspartate with no taurine; Pharma Nord adds 50 mg taurine per tablet. All three RCTs had Horphag-linked investigators, Tian 2023 rated their risk of bias low, unclear and high, and Horphag itself reports that the benefit faded after stopping.
  - Label conflict: after day 14 the label dose (2 tablets a day) equals Aoki 2012's low dose, which did not beat placebo on the erectile domain. Staying on 4 tablets a day goes against the label's 'do not exceed recommended amount'. Germany and Austria sell it as a food for special medical purposes, for use under medical supervision, so the 4-tablet plan needs a doctor.
  - Stacking: taken alongside the drive picks, the glucose-lowering effects (Pycnogenol, arginine, fenugreek, ashwagandha) and anti-platelet effects (Pycnogenol, arginine, fenugreek) add up. Monitor glucose on insulin or sulfonylureas, and watch for bleeding on aspirin or anticoagulants.
  - It is uncertified, so the absence of hidden PDE5 drugs is untested, although the prior risk is low for a Danish maker producing to pharmaceutical standards. Pharma Nord's record with the FDA, MHRA and EU RASFF could not be re-queried this pass because the hosts are blocked.

#### Verification record 2 of 3

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — This is the closest like-for-like replacement for Life Extension Prelox (the same licensed tablet with every milligram printed, and 4 tablets a day reproduces the RCT dose exactly), but it stays at 'acceptable' because no certifier lists it, its freedom from hidden drugs rests on reputation rather than testing, and its UK/EU regulatory record could not be fully checked in this run.
- **Product as verified:** Pharma Nord (Horphag Research licensee) — Pharma Nord Prelox tablets (60 or 180), UK/EU
- **Product page:** https://www.pharmanord.co.uk/all-products/mens-health/prelox
- **Where sold:** UK: Pharma Nord UK direct and Holland & Barrett. EU: Horphag names Pharma Nord as Prelox licensee for the UK, Germany, Austria, Belgium, Finland, Lithuania, Netherlands, Portugal and Sweden (https://www.prelox.com/shop). Retailers were found in Germany and Austria (as a food for special medical purposes), the Netherlands (Vitaminstore), France (Redcare) and Estonia. Gulf/Middle East: no retailer found in the UAE, Saudi Arabia, Qatar or Kuwait. Pharma Nord's careers page mentions a Middle East presence via subsidiaries and partners, but its country selector lists no GCC state. US: not sold; Life Extension holds the US licence.
- **Label verified:** yes. Pharma Nord UK panel, captured verbatim on 7 Nov 2026 in project notes (https://www.pharmanord.co.uk/all-products/mens-health/prelox). It matches the Holland & Barrett per-tablet panel, the pharmanord.eu page and the German leaflet (https://www.rathaus-apotheken.de/pimg/docs/00089537.pdf), all read via the search index on 8 Nov 2026. The .eu table omits taurine while the UK, Dutch and German copies list it, so check the pack.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Per 2 tablets: 'Prelox Proprietary Blend 1540mg | L-Arginine aspartate 1400mg | Taurine 100mg | Pycnogenol 40mg'. Every component is itemised (1,400 + 100 + 40 = 1,540 mg), so nothing is hidden. Per tablet: 700 mg arginine aspartate, 50 mg taurine, 20 mg Pycnogenol. Directions: '2 tablets twice per day for the first 2 weeks and 1 tablet twice per day thereafter ... Do not exceed recommended amount. To be taken with food.' Days 1-14 (4 tablets): arginine aspartate 2,800 mg (about 1.59 g free arginine; my calculation, 174.2/307.3), taurine 200 mg, Pycnogenol 80 mg. Thereafter (2 tablets): 1,400 mg (about 0.79 g arginine), 100 mg and 40 mg. Ingredients (UK): arginine aspartate, microcrystalline cellulose, silicon dioxide, taurine, mono- and diglycerides of fatty acids, Pycnogenol, croscarmellose sodium, magnesium stearate, glazing agents (hydroxypropyl cellulose, HPMC, talc, calcium carbonate) and spirulina colour. Vegetarian and vegan per Holland & Barrett. No stimulant or hormone.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** Ledda 2010 (124 men, 6 months) and Stanislavov 2008 (50 men, crossover) used 2 tablets twice daily of a tablet holding 20 mg Pycnogenol + 700 mg L-arginine aspartate: 80 mg + 2.8-3 g/day (Tian 2023 Table 1, https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/). That is this product's per-tablet composition, plus 50 mg taurine, which the trial descriptions do not mention. The label's first 2 weeks (4/day) match exactly. Its maintenance dose (2/day) is half the trial dose, close to Aoki 2012's low-dose regimen, whose erectile domain did not beat placebo. To reproduce Ledda and Stanislavov, stay on 4 tablets/day, the label's own starting dose; continuing it exceeds the maintenance direction, so agree it with a doctor. Free arginine at 4 tablets (about 1.6 g) is below the 2.5-6 g of arginine-only trials, so the effect rests on the combination.
- **Certification:** None. 'Manufactured to pharmaceutical standards' and Pharma Nord's own GMP plant in Vojens, Denmark are company claims, not certifier listings. Prelox is absent from NSF's Certified for Sport directory (live scrape of 2,625 products, 8 Nov 2026), and discovery searches found no Informed Sport/Informed Choice, BSCG, USP or NSF/ANSI 173 listing. None of these could be re-queried this run. (certifier page: none; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Pharma Nord ApS has its headquarters in Vejle and its plant in Vojens, Denmark. It says it produces under GMP 'under the auspices of the Danish Medicines Agency' (https://shop.pharmanord.eu/welcome/produced-in-denmark), but no Danish manufacturing authorisation for Pharma Nord was found to confirm this. In Germany and Austria Prelox is a food for special medical purposes ('ergänzende bilanzierte Diät') for the dietary management of ED, to be used by adults under medical supervision (https://www.onlineapo.at/en/Prelox-tablets/RA5982982). A web search on 8 Nov 2026 for Pharma Nord recalls, warning letters or withdrawals returned only other Danish firms (Novo Nordisk, Europharma, Scanpharm), nothing naming Pharma Nord. No Prelox entry appears on FDA's 441-entry tainted sexual-enhancement list (prior project checks). Not searched: EU RASFF, MHRA/FSA, the Danish Medicines Agency register, Health Canada, TGA. Live re-checks were impossible this pass (WebSearch limit used up, WebFetch blocked for pharmanord.co.uk and others, Firecrawl unavailable). No red flag is known, but the check is incomplete.
- **Evidence summary:** There are three small double-blind RCTs of this formula, all with Horphag-linked authors. Ledda 2010 (124 men aged 30-50, 6 months): IIEF erectile domain 15.2 to 25.2 at 3 months and 27.1 at 6 months, versus 19.1 and 19.0 on placebo; testosterone rose from 15.9 to 18.9 nmol/L (https://europepmc.org/article/MED/20184576). Stanislavov 2008 (crossover): erectile function normalised and intercourse frequency doubled (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17703218/). Aoki 2012 (low dose): IIEF-5 total improved (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21618639/). The Tian 2023 meta-analysis found IIEF-EF +8.9 (95% CI 4.14-13.66; 2 trials) with no adverse events, but risk of bias unclear to high. Horphag says the benefit faded after stopping (https://www.prelox.com/research). There are no data beyond 6 months, over age 50 or in severe ED. The EAU does not endorse Prelox.

**Safety risks**

- Vasodilation: headache, flushing, dizziness or palpitations (Drugs.com). The German leaflet warns of initial facial flushing or headache that usually settles.
- Recent heart attack: arginine 9 g/day after STEMI was stopped for excess deaths, 8.6% vs 0% (VINTAGE MI); Mayo Clinic advises against arginine after a recent heart attack. This product gives about 1.6 g/day, but the caution stands.
- Bleeding: Pycnogenol inhibits platelet aggregation (Putter 1999; MSKCC), although 27+ studies reported no increased bleeding (Examine).
- High potassium with potassium-sparing diuretics (Mayo Clinic). Mild GI upset or insomnia is possible (Gallo 2020).
- Taurine at 100-200 mg/day is far below EFSA's no-adverse-effect level of 1,000 mg/kg/day.
- No drug-screening certification, so the absence of hidden PDE5 inhibitors is not independently tested, though no adulteration report exists for Prelox.
- An unverified customer review on Pharma Nord's UK site reports a 5-hour erection that needed A&E. There are no safety data beyond 6 months.

**Interactions**

- Nitrates and NO donors (nitroglycerin, isosorbide, nicorandil, 'poppers'): do not combine; additive hypotension (Mayo Clinic; Drugs.com).
- PDE5 inhibitors: additive blood-pressure lowering, Drugs.com 'moderate'; arginine 2.5 g + tadalafil 5 mg caused no serious events in 300 men (Gallo 2020).
- Antihypertensives and alpha-blockers (tamsulosin, doxazosin): additive hypotension (Cleveland Clinic; Drugs.com).
- Potassium-sparing diuretics: hyperkalaemia (Mayo Clinic).
- Anticoagulants/antiplatelets (warfarin, DOACs, clopidogrel, aspirin, NSAIDs): added bleeding risk (Mayo Clinic; MSKCC).
- Diabetes drugs: may lower glucose (Mayo Clinic; Examine). Isoproterenol: hypotension (Mayo Clinic).
- Thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: none documented for arginine, Pycnogenol or taurine in the sources reviewed.

**Who should avoid it**

- Nitrate or NO-donor users, including 'poppers'.
- Men within 6 months of a heart attack, stroke or serious arrhythmia, or with unstable angina, heart failure, hypotension or uncontrolled hypertension (VINTAGE MI; sildenafil label).
- Men on anticoagulants or antiplatelets, or with a bleeding disorder, unless a doctor agrees; stop before surgery.
- Men on potassium-sparing diuretics or with kidney disease or an electrolyte imbalance (Drugs.com).
- Men with a pine allergy or GAMT deficiency (Cleveland Clinic); caution with asthma (Mayo Clinic).
- Per the German leaflet, men with a serious illness, severe hypertension or heart problems, or on many medicines, should consult a doctor first; it is meant for adults under medical supervision.
- Men with new, unexplained ED who have not had a cardiovascular check (AUA).

**Stop signals**

- Chest pain, palpitations, fainting or marked dizziness.
- An erection lasting more than 4 hours: emergency care.
- Unusual bruising, nosebleeds, or blood in urine or stool.
- Headache, flushing or stomach upset persisting beyond 2 weeks, or a rash or swelling.
- Low-blood-sugar symptoms if on diabetes drugs.
- A gain of less than 4 IIEF erectile-domain points (2 if ED is mild) after 8-12 weeks at the trial dose (Rosen 2011).

- **Product as verified:** Pharma Nord (Horphag Research licensee) — Prelox tablets, UK/EU version (60 or 180 tablets)

The panel says 'blend', but every component is itemised (1,400 + 100 + 40 = 1,540 mg), so nothing is hidden.
Per tablet: L-arginine aspartate 700 mg, taurine 50 mg, Pycnogenol 20 mg.
Directions: 2 tablets twice daily for the first 2 weeks, then 1 tablet twice daily, with food.
- Days 1-14 (4 tablets): L-arginine aspartate 2,800 mg (about 1.6 g free L-arginine by molecular weight, 174.2/307.3), taurine 200 mg, Pycnogenol 80 mg.
- After that (2 tablets): arginine aspartate 1,400 mg (about 0.8 g arginine), taurine 100 mg, Pycnogenol 40 mg.
Other ingredients: microcrystalline cellulose, silicon dioxide, mono- and diglycerides of fatty acids, croscarmellose sodium, magnesium stearate, glazing agents (hydroxypropyl cellulose, hydroxypropyl methylcellulose, talc, calcium carbonate) and concentrated spirulina colour. No stimulant, hormone or other herb.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** Trial regimen: Ledda 2010 (124 men aged 30-50, moderate ED, 6 months) and Stanislavov 2008 (50 men, 1-month crossover) gave 2 tablets twice daily of a tablet holding 20 mg Pycnogenol + 700 mg L-arginine aspartate. That is 80 mg Pycnogenol + 2.8-3 g arginine aspartate a day [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/; https://europepmc.org/article/MED/20184576; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17703218/]. Pharma Nord's tablet has the same two actives, plus 50 mg taurine, which the trial descriptions do not mention.
- Label days 1-14 (4 tablets/day) = 80 mg + 2,800 mg: an exact match.
- Label maintenance (2 tablets/day) = 40 mg + 1,400 mg: half the trial dose. This is close to Aoki 2012's low-dose regimen (60 mg Pycnogenol + 690 mg arginine + 552 mg aspartic acid for 8 weeks), which did not beat placebo on the erectile domain [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21618639/].
To reproduce Ledda and Stanislavov, a user would stay on 4 tablets a day, the label's own starting dose. That goes beyond the label's maintenance direction, so agree it with a doctor.
Free L-arginine at 4 tablets a day (about 1.6-1.7 g) is below the 2.5-6 g/day used in arginine-only trials, so the reported effect depends on the combination [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/12851125].

The workflow's discovery searches found no product-level listing in Informed Sport/Informed Choice, BSCG, NSF or USP, and the product is absent from the 2,625-listing NSF Certified for Sport directory captured on 8 Nov 2026 [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/]. 'Manufactured to pharmaceutical standards' and Pharma Nord's own GMP plant in Vojens, Denmark, are company claims, not certification. No third party screens this product for hidden drugs. Horphag Research owns the Prelox and Pycnogenol trademarks and names Pharma Nord as its European Prelox licensee [https://www.prelox.com/shop]. In Germany and Austria, Prelox is sold as a food for special medical purposes for the dietary management of ED [https://www.onlineapo.at/en/Prelox-tablets/RA5982982].
Regulatory record: none found.

Not searched: MHRA/FSA, EU RASFF, the Danish Veterinary and Food Administration, Health Canada and TGA. They were unreachable this session: the web-search budget was used up, the network policy blocked them, and Firecrawl credits had run out until 9 Nov 2026 05:26 UTC.
Status: no red flag known, but the check is incomplete.
- **Evidence summary:** There are three small double-blind RCTs of this two-ingredient formula, all with Horphag-linked authors:
- Ledda 2010: the IIEF erectile domain rose from 15.2 to 25.2 at 3 months and 27.1 at 6 months, vs 15.1 to 19.1 and 19.0 on placebo; total testosterone rose from 15.9 to 18.9 nmol/L [https://europepmc.org/article/MED/20184576].
- Stanislavov 2008 (crossover): erectile function was restored to normal in 1 month, with no unwanted effects [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17703218/].
- Aoki 2012 (lower dose): the IIEF-5 total improved, but the erectile domain was not significantly better than placebo [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21618639/].
The 2023 meta-analysis (3 RCTs, 184 men) found IIEF-EF +8.9 points vs placebo (95% CI 4.14-13.66; 2 trials) and no adverse events, but rated the three trials' risk of bias as low, unclear and high [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/]. In my reading, a gain that large is hard to believe next to the +3-4 points for arginine alone, and no independent group has replicated it.
Horphag says the benefit largely faded after stopping in the crossover study [https://www.prelox.com/mobile/research]. There are no data beyond 6 months, in men over 50 or in severe ED. The EAU does not endorse Prelox [https://uroweb.org/guidelines/sexual-and-reproductive-health/chapter/management-of-erectile-dysfunction].

**Safety risks**

- Vasodilation effects at the start: Pharma Nord's leaflet warns that some users may at first get facial flushing or headache. It advises seeing a doctor first if you have heart problems or severe high blood pressure, or take many medicines [https://www.rathaus-apotheken.de/pimg/docs/00089537.pdf; https://www.onlineapo.at/en/Prelox-tablets/RA5982982].
- Recent heart attack: an RCT of 9 g/day arginine after STEMI was stopped for excess deaths (8.6% vs 0%) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/], and Mayo Clinic advises against arginine after a recent heart attack [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681]. Prelox gives far less arginine (about 1.6 g/day), but the caution still applies.
- Bleeding: Pycnogenol inhibits platelet aggregation; 100 mg matched 500 mg aspirin against smoking-induced aggregation [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10498385/; https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/pine-bark-extract]. Examine notes no increase in bleeding across 27 Pycnogenol studies [https://examine.com/supplements/pycnogenol/].
- High blood potassium if combined with potassium-sparing diuretics [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Mild GI upset, insomnia, headache and dyspepsia: 11 adverse events per 100 men over 12 weeks at 2.5 g/day arginine, with insomnia in 5% [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/].
- Possible prolonged erection: one unverified customer review on Pharma Nord's UK page reports a 5-hour erection that needed a hospital emergency visit [https://www.pharmanord.co.uk/all-products/mens-health/prelox].
- Taurine at 100-200 mg/day is far below EFSA's no-observed-adverse-effect level of 1,000 mg/kg body weight per day [https://www.efsa.europa.eu/en/news/efsa-adopts-opinion-two-ingredients-commonly-used-some-energy-drinks].
- No drug-screening certification, so no independent test rules out hidden PDE5 inhibitors, although no adulteration report exists for Prelox.
- No safety data beyond 6 months of continuous use [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10614297/].

**Interactions**

- Nitrates and NO donors (nitroglycerin, isosorbide mono- or dinitrate, nicorandil, amyl nitrite 'poppers'): additive hypotension, so do not combine [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine-with-sildenafil-232-0-2061-0.html?professional=1].
- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil, vardenafil): additive blood-pressure lowering; Drugs.com rates arginine + sildenafil as moderate (monitor). In a 300-man trial, arginine 2.5 g + tadalafil 5 mg daily caused more mild side effects but no serious events [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7261690/].
- Antihypertensives (ACE inhibitors, ARBs, beta-blockers, calcium-channel blockers) and alpha-blockers such as tamsulosin or doxazosin: additive hypotension [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine; https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/l-arginine-with-tamsulosin-232-4042-2146-0.html?professional=1].
- Potassium-sparing diuretics (spironolactone, amiloride, triamterene): risk of high blood potassium [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (warfarin, apixaban, rivaroxaban, clopidogrel, aspirin, NSAIDs): higher bleeding risk, from both arginine and pine bark [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/pine-bark-extract].
- Diabetes drugs (insulin, sulfonylureas, metformin): arginine and Pycnogenol may lower blood glucose, so doses may need adjusting [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681; https://examine.com/supplements/pycnogenol/].
- Isoproterenol: hypotension [https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Thyroid drugs, SSRIs, MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction documented for arginine, Pycnogenol or taurine in the sources reviewed (Mayo Clinic, Drugs.com, MSKCC, Examine).

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone taking nitrates or other NO donors, including recreational 'poppers'.
- Men within 6 months of a heart attack, stroke or life-threatening arrhythmia, and men with unstable angina or heart failure [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16391217/; https://www.drugs.com/pro/viagra.html].
- Men with low resting blood pressure or uncontrolled high blood pressure. Pharma Nord's leaflet says to consult a doctor first with severe hypertension or heart problems, or if taking many medicines [https://www.rathaus-apotheken.de/pimg/docs/00089537.pdf].
- Men on anticoagulants or antiplatelets, or with a bleeding disorder, unless a doctor agrees. Stop before planned surgery and tell the surgeon [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/pine-bark-extract].
- Men on potassium-sparing diuretics, or with kidney disease or an electrolyte imbalance [https://www.drugs.com/drug-interactions/arginine,l-arginine.html].
- People with guanidinoacetate methyltransferase deficiency; caution with asthma or allergies, including pine allergy [https://my.clevelandclinic.org/health/drugs/22536-l-arginine; https://www.mayoclinic.org/drugs-supplements-l-arginine/art-20364681].
- Men with new, unexplained ED who have not had a cardiovascular check; the AUA calls ED a risk marker for cardiovascular disease [https://www.auanet.org/guidelines-and-quality/guidelines/erectile-dysfunction-(ed)-guideline].

**Stop signals**

- Chest pain, palpitations, fainting or marked dizziness: stop and get medical care.
- An erection lasting more than 4 hours: get emergency care [https://www.drugs.com/pro/viagra.html].
- Unusual bruising, nosebleeds, or blood in urine or stool.
- Headache, flushing or stomach upset that continues beyond the first 2 weeks.
- Symptoms of low blood sugar if you take diabetes medicines.
- Rash, swelling or another allergic reaction.
- No gain of at least 4 points on the IIEF erectile domain (2 points if ED is mild) after 8-12 weeks at the trial dose: stop, because it is not working [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21855209/].

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).

'Manufactured to pharmaceutical standards' and the own GMP plant are company claims.
  - Label confirmed from the raw 7 Nov 2026 capture of https://www.pharmanord.co.uk/all-products/mens-health/prelox: per 2 tablets L-arginine aspartate 1,400 mg, taurine 100 mg, Pycnogenol 40 mg (the 1,540 mg 'blend' is fully itemised); 2 tablets twice daily for 2 weeks, then 1 tablet twice daily, with food; 'Do not exceed recommended amount'.
  - Staying on the trial regimen (4 tablets a day) goes beyond the label's maintenance direction and its 'do not exceed' line, so it needs a doctor's agreement.
  - No reformulation evidence; the pharmanord.eu table omits taurine while the UK and German copies list it, so check the pack in hand.

- *safety-evidence lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*
  - Missed caution: monographs based on Natural Medicines (WebMD) list an immunosuppressant interaction and advise avoiding Pycnogenol in autoimmune disease (theoretical immune stimulation). From my own knowledge; not re-opened this pass.
  - The trial tablets contained no taurine, and all three RCTs were Horphag-linked. The entry's own scepticism about the +8.9-point gain is warranted.
  - Continuing 4 tablets a day beyond day 14 is off-label ('do not exceed recommended amount'), while the label maintenance dose matches Aoki's dose, which failed. The 4-tablet plan therefore needs medical supervision.
  - Stacking with the drive picks adds glucose-lowering and anti-platelet effects. Monitor glucose on insulin or sulfonylureas, and watch for bleeding on aspirin or anticoagulants.
